# IMPL-09_calibration

# SPEC 09 — Confidence Calibration

> Aligned with the code of 2026-10-23.

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-01, IMPL-02, IMPL-07, IMPL-08. Uses the inference core (IMPL-07) for logprobs/spans and the evaluation normalizer (IMPL-08) for the correctness signal. No forward dependency on serving/testing.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.7.docx` §5 (**confidence flow, calibration, list-field second signal**), §0b (LoB carries confidence too), §15 (ECE as a gating metric).

## Goal

Turn raw token logprobs into trustworthy per-field confidence via post-hoc calibration, plus the list-field completeness signal that logprobs are structurally blind to.

## The end-to-end flow being implemented (arch §5)

```
Generation ──> token logprobs ──> map to field spans ──> aggregate per field
                                                              │
                                                        raw confidence
                                                              │
                                              post-hoc calibration transform
                                              (temperature / isotonic, per type)
                                                              │
                                                     calibrated confidence
                                                       │              │
                                              scalar fields      list fields
                                                       │              │
                                              threshold check   + row-completeness
                                                       │              │
                                                       └──> review routing <──┘
```

(This is v1's flow. Under arch v2.1 the "aggregate per field" and "calibration transform" steps are a feature vector per field and a per-field-type calibrator, and the threshold is fitted per field type — see *Current implementation*.)

**Why calibration is not optional:** fine-tuned generative models — especially after LoRA fine-tuning on a narrow task — become **overconfident**. Correct and incorrect outputs carry similarly high token probabilities, because the model has learned to be fluent in the target format even when it is wrong about the content. Raw logprobs alone are not a usable confidence signal.

**Why not a separate verifier model:** another model to train, version, evaluate, and keep in sync with every Foundation/adapter version, plus doubled inference latency. It stays a documented escalation path **if** calibrated logprobs prove insufficient in evaluation (IMPL-08 ECE) — start with the cheap, well-understood approach.

## Deliverables

### 1. `calibration/logprob_confidence.py`
- Consumes field spans + logprobs from IMPL-07 `span_map` and aggregates to a raw per-field confidence.
- **Superseded as the confidence itself (arch v2.1 §5.1).** The minimum token probability is length-biased — the minimum of *n* draws falls as *n* grows, so a long correct policy number scores below a short wrong year. It is now **one feature among several** handed to a per-field-type calibrator; see *Current implementation* below. Serving reads this raw aggregate only when it holds no calibrator set, and then flags every field.
- Handles nested fields and list fields (per-value confidence within each row).
- **`line_of_business` gets confidence like any other field** (arch §0b) and is emitted in the same `{value, confidence}` shape.
- Costs nothing extra at training or inference time — the logprobs are already there.

### 2. `calibration/fit_calibration.py`
- On a **held-out labeled validation set — never training data** — compare raw confidence against actual field-level correctness (exact / normalized match via `common.normalize` from IMPL-08) and fit a transform:
  - **`temperature` scaling** (default: simple, one parameter, works well here)
  - **`isotonic` regression** (more flexible; handles non-monotonic miscalibration)
- Fit **per doc_type**, optionally per field-type. Fit for a specific model version — calibration is not transferable across versions.
- Persist to `calibration/{version}/{doc_type}.json` in Blob (`paths.calibration_params`, IMPL-02). This is a small lookup/transform applied at serving time **after** the model call, before the JSON is returned.
- Report the ECE before and after fitting, so a fit that fails to improve calibration is visible rather than assumed.

### 3. `calibration/apply_calibration.py`
- Load the fitted transform for a model version + doc_type; map raw → calibrated confidence at inference time. Used by serving (IMPL-11) and testing (IMPL-12).
- **Fails loudly when no calibration exists for a version/doc_type** rather than silently passing raw (overconfident) values through as if calibrated. A missing calibration is an operational error, not a fallback.
- Applies the review threshold (default 0.7, `DEFAULT_REVIEW_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD` — arch §5) and emits `review_flags` for sub-threshold fields, which is the practical payoff of doing calibration properly. On serving's v1 fallback path no field is accepted on it (no measured error rate stands behind 0.7).

### 4. `calibration/list_completeness.py`
The second confidence signal that per-token logprobs structurally cannot provide (arch §5).

**The failure mode:** if the model extracts 6 of 8 claims, the 2 missing claims have **no generated tokens**, so there is no low probability to flag. Per-field confidence on the 6 extracted rows can all be high while the extraction is silently incomplete. This is a recall failure, and token confidence is blind to it.

Implement **two independent cross-checks**, either of which flags the list:
1. **Document-stated count** — a `total claims: N` style field extracted from the document.
2. **Structure-derived count** — the number of table rows MinerU detected on the relevant pages (recorded in `ocr_meta.json` by IMPL-03).

When the model's row count disagrees with either, **flag the whole list for review regardless of per-value confidence**.

**The flag format is a contract, so it is a constant.** Flags are `f"{field}{ROW_MISMATCH_SUFFIX}"` — `"claims:row_count_mismatch"` — and `is_row_completeness_flag()` is the only supported way to recognise one. Both are exported from this module and imported by `data_pipeline.labeling.active_learning`, which has to recognise them to honour the "row completeness outranks every confidence score" rule. The consumer previously matched hand-written prefixes (`list:`, `rows:`, `completeness:`) that this module has never emitted, so the override was dead code and an incomplete Loss Run with high per-value confidence was routed to a spot check.

**A stated count is read however the model spelled it.** `8`, `8.0` and `"8"` are the same assertion; accepting only `int` turned the whole stated-count cross-check off whenever the model emitted a JSON string.

**A flagged list is never fully confident.** `row_completeness_confidence` is scored against whichever signal disagrees *most*, not the first one available — preferring the stated count meant a list flagged solely by the structural check (document says 6, model returns 6, OCR saw 9) shipped confidence `1.0` on a list this function had just flagged. Optionally calibrate a separate `row_completeness_confidence` against ground-truth row counts on the validation set.

This matters most for **Loss Runs**, where a missed claim row is both easy to make and expensive to miss.

## Constraints
- Calibration fit ONLY on held-out data — assert the validation `source_id`s are disjoint from training.
- Params versioned per model version + doc_type (v2.1: per release + serving format); never reused across versions.
- Correctness signal reuses IMPL-08's definition of "correct" — `common.normalize` through `evaluation.metrics.field_accuracy.values_agree` (by the field's declared type) — the same definition as the promotion gate.
- Raw confidence is never returned to callers as if it were calibrated.
- No PII in logs (confidence records reference field paths, not field values).

## Acceptance checklist
- [ ] Raw per-field confidence extracted from a sample generation + logprobs (via IMPL-07 spans), including a `claims[i].amount` row field and `line_of_business`.
- [ ] Aggregation strategy is configurable and the choice is recorded.
- [ ] Temperature and isotonic both reduce ECE on a synthetic miscalibrated set; before/after ECE is reported.
- [ ] Fitted params persist/reload and apply deterministically.
- [ ] `apply_calibration` **raises** when no calibration exists for a version/doc_type.
- [ ] `list_completeness` flags a list when extracted rows < the document-stated count, and separately when < the MinerU-detected row count.
- [ ] A flagged list is routed to review even when every per-value confidence is high.
- [ ] Calibrated confidence is available with **no ground truth** — the inference-time case that makes production review routing possible.
- [ ] Fitting on data overlapping the training split fails the assertion.
- [ ] The calibrators record the checkpoint they were fitted on, and calibrators of another checkpoint than the one selected now are refused when read back.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-21)

This spec's §1–§3 describe v1's single-number calibration. What runs now (arch v2.1 §5):

1. **Features per field** (`calibration/features.py::build_document_features`): min / mean / first-token
   logprob, token count, **OCR agreement**, **rule checks**, null flag, cross-mode agreement, field type. A
   feature that was not computed encodes as a neutral 0.5 plus a presence flag, never as 0.
   - *The logprob features read the printed value's own tokens*: a string's characters without its quotes
     (`inference_core.span_map`), and for a canonical envelope the span of `raw` — the reading of the page —
     with `parsed`'s standing in only when nothing was read (`common.canonical.collapse_spans`). `parsed` is
     the same value reformatted, written after `raw` and near-certain whether the reading was right or not.
   - *OCR agreement* compares the page text against the value **as printed** (a canonical envelope's
     `raw`, via `printed_view`), not the normalised `parsed` — a date rewritten to `MM/DD/YYYY` is not on the
     page.
   - *Grounded on the cited page*: given each page's text (`page_texts`, page number → text), agreement is
     `page_agreement` — `common.grounding.ground` against the pages the value's envelope cites: **1.0** printed
     on a cited page, **0.5** printed only on other pages (a wrong page, or another row's figure), **0.0**
     printed on no page, `None` when a cited page has no text (a scan OCR could not read), the value is too
     short to look for (under 3 characters), or there is no text (image_only). Serving and calibration
     fitting both pass each page's text. Without `page_texts`, `ocr_agreement` searches the whole shown text
     with the same matching — word boundaries, every word across line breaks, figures in any format — so "1"
     no longer agrees with every page holding a 1.
   - *Page text* has ONE definition for fitting and serving (`shown_ocr_text`): the OCR text of the pages
     the model was shown, page markers and the blank-page placeholder removed, `None` for `image_only`.
   - *Rule checks* read siblings beside the field (`policy.effective_date` vs `policy.expiration_date`),
     so they fire on canonical policies; totals against their rows; identifier shape.
   - *Field type* (`infer_field_type`) — one of `identifier`, `money`, `date`, `number`, `entity`, `address`,
     `enum`, `free_text`: the reviewed table `configs/field_types.yaml` first; then, **for a common-model
     document only**, the type its schema declares (`common_model_field_types` over `VALUE_TYPE_FIELD_TYPES`:
     `MoneyValue` money, `DateValue` date, year/number/percent number, NAIC and postal code identifier,
     state and the enum-like values enum) and an `additional_fields` value by what was parsed; then the name
     (`common.normalize.infer_field_kind`; an address by any path segment, `carrier.address.city`). The
     self-contained lines and ACORD keep the name-based types for the paths they share with the common model.
   - Features are built on the **value view** of the extraction (envelopes collapsed), keyed like serving.
     A field whose span could not be located is kept with `mapped=False` (no confidence, reviewed), never
     dropped.
2. **Per-field-type calibrators** (`calibration/feature_calibrator.py`): logistic regression per field type
   over those features (pure Python, L2-regularised), fitted on the **calibration half** of validation. A type
   with fewer than `MIN_INSTANCES` (300) labelled instances, or with only one outcome, gets no enforced
   calibrator, and every field of it routes to review. Isotonic above `ISOTONIC_THRESHOLD` (2,000) is
   recorded, not implemented.
3. **Risk-controlled thresholds** (`calibration/thresholds.py`): per field type, the lowest threshold on the
   0.50–0.99 grid whose auto-accepted error rate, as the **upper bound of a 95% binomial interval**, meets the
   target with at least `MIN_ACCEPTED_SAMPLE` (30) accepted fields, chosen on the **threshold half**.
   `DEFAULT_ERROR_TARGETS`: identifier, money, date, number 1%; enum 2%; entity, address 5%; free text has no
   target and is never auto-accepted. A type with no calibrator, or no threshold meeting the target, routes
   to review rather than carrying an unmeasured number.
4. **Per serving format**: each format gets its own calibrators from its own generations (quantization
   moves the logprob distribution), stored at
   `releases/{tenant}/{release}/calibration/{format}/calibrators.json` (`paths.release_calibrators`; the
   calibrators and the thresholds together, with `adapter_checkpoint`: the checkpoint whose generations they
   were fitted on, the selected one, else the staged adapter directory).
5. **Loss Run reconciliation** (`calibration/reconciliation.py`) per policy period, alongside row
   completeness.

**Where the samples come from.** `orchestration.pipeline_dag.collect_calibration_samples` reads the adapter's
validation split (`val/scope/policy/val.jsonl`), localises its page images, and for each serving format in turn
(`bf16` only since 2026-10-23: nothing is merged or quantized) loads the model as it is served — the base with
the selected adapter applied (`load_model("base", adapter=...)`) — generates **every validation row** with
`evaluation.validation_generation.generate_validation` (constrained as serving is, `page_ref` bounded to each
row's pages, the row's task answer cap, `validation_batch_rows` rows per call — IMPL-08), releases the engine
before the next format loads, and logs what the pass cost: rows, seconds, calls, rows per call and the slowest
call. A pass with too many failed rows is refused (`assert_generations_usable`); a format with no evidence gets
no entry. A resumed `package` finds the calibrators in Blob (`pipeline_dag.release_calibration`), and refuses
those fitted on another checkpoint than the one selected now, with "Run package again from calibrate":
confidence from one checkpoint's logprobs says nothing about another's.

**How a sample is labelled** (`validation_generation.calibration_samples`), by the `val_half` the dataset build
stamped on each row (a row without one is left out, with a warning):
- a policy read in windows is fitted **as it is served**: its windows and their spans merged
  (`merged_windows`, the serving merge), held to the label conventions (`serving.conventions.conform`), then
  featured against each page's text and scored against the merged window targets — not one window at a time.
  The windows cover every page of the document (decision 2026-10-19; IMPL-05), as serving reads it, so the
  calibrators are fitted on whole policies merged as served, never on a selection of pages;
- every other row is fitted as generated; a row that failed to generate has no features;
- correctness is `values_agree` (IMPL-08's typed comparison), with the label's rows **paired by identity** to
  the answer's (`aligned_for_scoring` on a common-model line, `rows_aligned_to` otherwise), so one omitted or
  reordered row does not make every later row's correct values "wrong";
- a common-model line is featured without its ids, codes and links (`without_bare_values`) and typed as its
  schema declares — what serving calibrates (`serving.pipeline._feature_calibrated`).

**The calibrate stage** (`stage_calibrate`) fits calibrators on the calibration half and thresholds on the
threshold half per format, writes them to the release, and reports per format the unenforced types, the
guarantees, `auto_accept_error_rate`, and the **expected calibration error per field type and overall**
(`expected_calibration_error`, keyed `all`) — both measured on the threshold half, which the calibrators were
not fitted on.

**Thresholds** load back for serving (`ThresholdSet.from_dict`). An explicit target table that leaves a
field type out now means *no promise* — every field of that type is reviewed — instead of silently falling
back to the default target.

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- Noted that the flow diagram is v1's and that serving's v1 path (no calibrator set) flags every field.
- Corrected the v1 persistence path to `calibration/{version}/{doc_type}.json` (`paths.calibration_params`).
- Constraints: correctness is `values_agree` (typed comparison), and v2.1 params are per release and serving format.
- Features: logprob features read the value's own tokens (no quotes; `raw`'s span via `collapse_spans`); OCR agreement grounded on the cited pages (`page_agreement`: 1.0 / 0.5 / 0.0 / none) with the shared `common.grounding` matcher; field types include `number` and the common model's declared value types (common-model documents only); neutral encoding of missing features.
- Calibrators and thresholds: added `MIN_INSTANCES` 300 and the single-outcome rule, the threshold grid, binomial upper bound, `MIN_ACCEPTED_SAMPLE` 30 and the per-type targets; the calibrators file name.
- Samples: described `collect_calibration_samples` (every val row per format, merged model, engine released between formats, rows/seconds/calls logged) and `calibration_samples` (windowed policies merged and conformed as served, rows paired by identity, typed correctness, common-model fields as serving calibrates them).
- Calibrate stage: reports ECE per field type and overall on the threshold half.

## Changes since 2026-10-13

- Header: aligned with the code of 2026-10-21; architecture reference is `finetuning-architecture-v2.4.docx`.
- Current implementation, *How a sample is labelled*: a windowed policy's windows cover every page (decision 2026-10-19), so calibrators are fitted on whole policies merged as served.

## Changes of 2026-10-22 (code review)

- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.5.docx` and the code of 2026-10-22.

## Changes of 2026-10-22 (one adapter)

- No training scopes (decision 2026-10-22): calibration samples come from the policy adapter's validation view.

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (incremental training)

- *Where the samples come from*: calibration loads the base with the selected adapter applied, as served, in bf16 only; the merged and quantized models are gone, and `--skip-quantize` with them (decision 2026-10-23).
- Code review of the incremental training: the calibrators record `adapter_checkpoint`, and calibrators fitted on another checkpoint are refused (*Current implementation*, item 4 and *Where the samples come from*; new acceptance item).

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (architecture v2.6)

- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.6.docx`, which now records the one policy adapter, one version per cycle, incremental training and no merge or quantization.

## Changes of 2026-10-24 (QA fixes)

- `margin_over_runner_up` is the selected checkpoint's sample F1 minus the best other candidate's, so it is negative when a whole-document pass or a tie-break chose the sample's runner-up; the low-margin warning reads it.
- The validation merge honours `window_leads`, as serving's merge does.

## Changes of 2026-10-24 (architecture v2.7)

- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.7.docx`, which records the decisions of 2026-10-23 and 2026-10-24: printed values only, consistent output, fields first, one MinerU, production on Azure.

## Changes of 2026-10-26 (pipeline review fixes)

- Thresholds by learn-then-test: tested from 0.99 down, the loosest that passed before the first failure (thresholds that could not pass even with no error are skipped), so the 95% promise holds.
- 12 features: the two cross-mode features (never filled) retired; `r_written` (the compact answer wrote its printed form), `near_tie` and `near_tie_present` (from `FieldSpan.top2_margin`) added; a column constant at fit time is left out. Old calibrators still load.
- Under fast, calibrators and thresholds are fitted on image_only readings with their document's text borrowed (`calibration.population`); ECE is reported per mode and field type. Standard fits every mode as before.
- `calibration.post_text.recalibrate_after_text` scores a served result's confidence again once the page text arrives; fields first runs it at the gate (cited pages) and at the end, and the Azure worker passes the release's calibrators into phase 2.

# IMPL-10_postprocessing_merge_quantize

# SPEC 10 — Postprocessing (Merge + Serving-Format Quantization)

> Aligned with the code of 2026-10-23.

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-02, IMPL-06.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.7.docx` §13 (no merge or quantize stage since 2026-10-23), §13a (**serving format: the adapter on the bf16 base**), §13b (quantization quality thresholds, not applied), §18 (artifact paths).

## Goal

Fold the trained adapter into the base model, and produce the quantized formats **the serving endpoint can actually load**. Quality thresholds are specified here and enforced before any quantized format is served.

**The pipeline no longer merges or quantizes** (decision 2026-10-23, with incremental training, IMPL-06). The release **is** the adapter: it is served as a LoRA on the bf16 base (`ReleaseBundle.served_as = "lora_on_base"`, IMPL-11), bf16 only, and calibration and the gate score the base with the selected adapter applied (`load_model("base", adapter=...)`) — what is served. The `merge` and `quantize` stages are removed; `package` pushes the adapter only and lost `--formats`, `--skip-quantize` and `--dtype`, and `orchestration/config/pipeline.yaml` defaults lost `formats` and `dtype`. `training/merge.py`, `postprocessing/quantize.py`, `quant_thresholds.py`, `validate_quant.py` and the GGUF export are kept and described below, but no stage calls them; §5 describes `package` as it runs now.

## What changed from v1, and why

v1 produced `fp16` + `q5_k_m` GGUF files every cycle. GGUF is **llama.cpp's** format and the endpoint runs **vLLM**, so the pipeline spent conversion and eval compute on an artifact nothing could deploy — and there was no small format the server *could* open, leaving bf16 or nothing.

v1's own module docstring had the right of it: *"GGUF is the portable/edge path and vLLM on the merged model is the primary one."* It never followed through, and left `DEFAULT_FORMATS = ("fp16", "q5_k_m")` making a GGUF the per-cycle deliverable.

**GPU only** (decision 2026-10-19). The merge, every quantized export and the vLLM engine that serves and scores them run on CUDA and refuse to start without it (`common/gpu.py` `require_cuda`); nothing falls back to the CPU. Every deployment target is a vLLM format on a GPU. GGUF stays an **optional edge export** (§2a), never a deployment target.

## 1. `training/merge.py`

*Not called by the pipeline since 2026-10-23: there is no merge stage.*

- Loads the base in **bf16** and folds in **one** adapter via PEFT `merge_and_unload()` → one standalone bf16 model.
- **One adapter, one merge.** v1 applied a Foundation LoRA and then a per-type LoRA on top, producing a model per document type — and the order mattered, because reversing it produced a different model from the one that was evaluated. That whole ordering problem is gone with the topology that created it (arch §4.1).
- A graduated per-type adapter (§4.2) is **never merged**. It is applied at serving time on top of these merged weights, one LoRA per request — the only shape vLLM can serve.
- Folds in the **checkpoint the §11.2 selector chose**, recorded on the plan (`MergePlan.selected_checkpoint`). `orchestration.pipeline_dag.stage_merge` reads it through `selected_checkpoint(ctx)`: this process's `checkpoint_eval` result, else the choice recorded in Blob at `eval-reports/v{n}/scope/policy/checkpoint_selection.json`. The merged weights do not say which checkpoint they came from, and once the staging volume is reclaimed nothing else does either.
- `dtype` defaults to **bf16, not fp16**: the adapter trained in bf16 against a bf16 base, and FP8 is quantized from this artifact. Merging to fp16 would insert a precision change between training and every serving format for no reason.
- Writes to the **staging volume**, under the adapter's name (`plan_merge(scope=ADAPTER.name)`): `staging/merged-models/scope/policy/v{n}/`. The merged model is ~16 GB and quantization also runs on RunPod; pushing it to Azure and pulling it back is a 32 GB round trip for nothing (master §12a).
- **Runs on the GPU** into the **pinned local base** (see *Current implementation*); written to `<output>.partial` and renamed into place only once `config.json` and the safetensors exist.

## 2. `postprocessing/quantize.py` — serving formats

*Not called by the pipeline since 2026-10-23: there is no quantize stage, and a release serves bf16 only. The table, the example and the rules below describe the code as it stands.*

| Format | Tool | Hardware | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| **bf16 merged** | PEFT merge | Any CUDA GPU | Reference, and cycle 1's serving format |
| **FP8 (W8A8)** | llm-compressor | Native FP8 on Ada/Hopper (L4, L40S, H100, H200); weight-only on A100 | Default serving format from cycle 2 |
| **AWQ INT4 (W4A16)** | llm-compressor | Modern NVIDIA | VRAM-constrained serving only, if it meets threshold |

```bash
# before 2026-10-23 only: --formats is gone, and package serves bf16 alone
# fp8 is refused until it is verified (below); --formats bf16 was the default
python -m orchestration.run package --version v3 --formats bf16 fp8      # the release id is the version
```

**bf16 is never re-exported.** It *is* the merged model; a copy would be a second 16 GB artifact identical to the first.

**With `--formats bf16` — the default (`defaults.formats` in `orchestration/config/pipeline.yaml`) — quantize is a no-op.** `_is_quantized` reports the stage complete as soon as merge is, and `quantize()` returns the merged model as the bf16 entry without exporting anything. (Until 2026-10-23: the stage, the flag and `defaults.formats` are gone.)

**FP8 is refused until verified.** `plan_quantization` defaults to bf16 alone and `quantize()` raises on `fp8` unless `fp8_verified=True` (on the plan; `StageContext.fp8_verified` went with the stage), which is to be set once Phase 0 spike item 9 has verified the export; no CLI flag sets it. An unverified serving format is a deployment that fails at load — or worse, one that loads and reads badly.

**The vision path is never quantized.** `NEVER_QUANTIZED = visual.*, mergers, aligners, lm_head`, passed to llm-compressor as its ignore list (arch §13a). Compressing it produces a model that loads cleanly and reads pages badly — very hard to notice, because it still emits well-formed JSON.

**Every serving format gets its own calibrator fit and its own gate run.** Quantization degrades exactly what was fine-tuned in, so an FP8 release inherits nothing from bf16's result. Today the evaluation gate scores bf16 only (its decision is `release_gate_decision(release, "bf16", tenant)`); a quantized format with no gate decision of its own leaves the release bundle at status `gated`, never `promoted` (`registry_utils.releases.promote_release` refuses it too).

## 2a. GGUF — the edge export, on request only

`plan_gguf_export` / `export_gguf` produce a GGUF for an **offline or edge** deployment. Not per-cycle, not on the serving path, not a deployment target (the deployment is GPU only), and **validated separately in llama.cpp** against the §13b AWQ INT4 column — the serving gate does not cover it, because the serving endpoint never loads one. No command calls it, and past its guards `export_gguf` raises `NotImplementedError`: the llama.cpp conversion is not wired.

The v1 mmproj guard is carried over **unchanged**: Qwen3-VL is multimodal, so a GGUF needs the quantized LLM **plus a separate `mmproj`** file for the vision encoder and projector. Exporting without a verified one produces a model that loads and cannot see, which fails silently on every image-only document.

Serving and edge artifacts are stored under **different runtime prefixes** — `quantized-models/scope/policy/v{n}/vllm/{format}/` and `.../gguf/{format}/`. They are loaded by different programs, and one prefix invites deploying the wrong one.

## 3. `postprocessing/quant_thresholds.py`

**Absolute percentage points against bf16, per field class** (arch §13b):

| Metric | FP8 max drop | AWQ INT4 max drop |
|---|---|---|
| Exact match — identifiers, money, dates | 0.5 pp | 1.0 pp |
| Match — names, addresses | 1.0 pp | 2.0 pp |
| List-field row recall | 0.5 pp | 1.0 pp |
| Confusable misattribution (increase) | 0.5 pp | 1.0 pp |
| ECE (increase) | 0.01 | 0.02 |
| Schema validity (constrained) | 100% | 100% |

`field_normalized_match` is judged on the **names, addresses** row and `field_exact_match` on the **identifiers, money, dates** row, each against its own allowance.

Three things about this table are deliberate:

- **Absolute, not relative.** v1 allowed a 2% *relative* field-F1 drop, which is 1.9 pp at F1 0.95 and 1.4 pp at 0.70 — so the rule got **stricter as the model got worse**, which is backwards. The same number should mean the same thing whatever the baseline.
- **Per field class.** A wrong policy number is a wrong extraction; a slightly-off entity name is usually still matchable. One allowance would let the forgiving class lend its slack to the unforgiving one.
- **Schema validity is a floor, not a margin.** Structured decoding guarantees it (§13), so anything below 100% means the guarantee is not working — an error, not a degraded result.

The reference is **bf16**. v1 measured every format against fp16, a GGUF format, so the reference itself was an artifact the endpoint could not load.

A format exceeding **any** threshold is not served. Asking the serving gate about a GGUF format raises and says where it *is* validated, rather than reading as "unsupported".

## 4. `postprocessing/validate_quant.py`

- Judges each produced format's metrics — from the extraction routine (IMPL-12) against the **frozen golden eval set** — against the table above. It scores nothing itself: the metrics are supplied (they came in as `StageContext.quant_metrics`, gone with the stage), and with none supplied the quantize stage logged a warning instead of judging. No CLI path supplies them; a bf16-only cycle has nothing to compare, because bf16 *is* the reference. No stage runs it since 2026-10-23.
- Also checks **row recall** and **confusable misattribution** on their own margins: a quantized model that keeps its field accuracy while dropping claim rows has lost exactly what a Loss Run is for, and an aggregate field score would not show it.
- Writes `quant_threshold_results` into the quantize stage result (`ValidationReport.manifest_entry()`) and **refuses promotion of an over-threshold format** (`assert_servable`) — no override flag.

## 5. Where the gates sit in `package`

`package` runs stages 7–9 in order (`orchestration.pipeline_dag.STAGES`): **calibrate** (IMPL-09) → **evaluation_gate** → **package** (the push of the adapter). There is no quantize stage, so the §4 threshold gate does not run (decision 2026-10-23).

- **The promotion gate** (IMPL-08) scores the base model with the selected adapter applied — as it is served, nothing merged — on the frozen golden eval set's policies of the lines the release serves, through serving, with the release's bf16 calibrators. It holds every floor; judges non-inferiority by a **paired bootstrap** over the per-document scores of the candidate's and the promoted release's eval reports (`paired_document_scores`, weighted as each metric counts its documents, and only on reports scored on the same frozen set of the tenant; otherwise a point comparison, with the reason in the decision's warnings); and blocks a release that improves no primary metric unless `--fixes-defect "<ticket>"` names the defect it fixes (on `package` and `all`, one value; recorded in the decision). The promoted release is the tenant's, read from its release index. A passing release is **production** at `field_normalized_match` ≥ 0.92 and **interim** below it — and **interim whatever its score** when it was scored on fewer than 150 real frozen policies, with a warning in the decision. A failed gate is passed only by a written override (`--override-approver`, `--override-reason`, `--override-gate`: all three), recorded as a waiver. A run that continued an adapter is also held, line by line, to the run it continued (IMPL-08).
- A blocked gate stops the release: the adapter stays on the staging volume, nothing is published, and the command exits non-zero with the per-metric verdicts. The push re-reads the recorded decision (`assert_gate_passed`) and refuses a version that did not pass, so a standalone `package` cannot publish it either. The release bundle carries the bf16 decision's tier. A release that passed is promoted by the push, and the tenant's previously promoted release archived (IMPL-13 §4) — never in a dry run, which pushes placeholders and leaves the bundle `gated`. `package` refuses a version whose release is already promoted or archived, before any work (a rollback is `promote`), and a release with no trained line (a dry run warns).
- **Latency is recorded, not gated** (no latency SLA; decision 2026-10-19): `record_release_measurements` copies P95 latency per adapter and peak GPU memory from the eval report's `release_measurements` onto the bundle, and a missing measurement is a warning, never a block.

## Constraints

- Merged models never overwrite adapters (separate Blob paths).
- Every **served** quantized format passes threshold validation first — no exceptions, no override.
- The bf16 reference is always produced: every §13b threshold is a margin against it, so a run producing only a quantized format has nothing to measure its drop from.
- Record all produced formats in the manifest.
- Never emit a GGUF whose multimodal projector support is unverified.

## Acceptance checklist

- [ ] `merge` produces one loadable bf16 model from one adapter, folding in the selected checkpoint.
- [ ] `plan_quantization` defaults to **bf16 alone**, and refuses a GGUF format with an error naming the runtime mismatch.
- [ ] `quantize` refuses FP8 until `fp8_verified`, and never re-exports bf16.
- [ ] The pipeline has no merge or quantize stage; `package` takes no `--formats`, `--skip-quantize` or `--dtype` and pushes the adapter only.
- [ ] A serving plan without the bf16 reference is refused outright.
- [ ] `NEVER_QUANTIZED` covers the vision tower, every merger and `lm_head`.
- [ ] `export_gguf` refuses an unverified mmproj, and is reachable only on request.
- [ ] Serving and GGUF artifacts land under different runtime prefixes.
- [ ] `quant_thresholds` returns pass/fail correctly **at each format's exact boundary** — a format landing precisely on its allowance passes.
- [ ] Margins behave identically at F1 0.95 and 0.70, which is what "absolute" means.
- [ ] `validate_quant` refuses an over-threshold format **with no override path**, and refuses validation without the bf16 reference.
- [ ] A format that was not measured does not pass.
- [ ] **`assert_servable` refuses a serving format that produced no verdict at all.** `validate_quant` only returns a result for formats it was given metrics for, so a format nobody scored appears in neither `servable_formats` nor `blocked_formats`. Checking `blocked_formats` alone let it sail through to push with zero measurements — the precise inverse of the rule this module exists to enforce.
- [ ] *(Retired 2026-10-23: no quantize stage.)* The threshold gate sat inside `package`, between quantize and calibrate.
- [ ] `package` publishes an adapter as its `adapter_config.json` and `adapter_model.safetensors` only, never a checkpoint's resume state.
- [ ] The merge and every quantized export refuse to run without CUDA; nothing falls back to the CPU.
- [ ] The promotion gate runs inside `package`, after calibrate and before the push; the push refuses a version whose recorded gate decision did not pass.
- [ ] The release bundle's tier is the bf16 gate decision's: `interim` below 0.92, and `interim` whatever the score when it was scored on fewer than 150 real frozen policies.
- [ ] P95 latency and peak GPU memory are recorded on the release bundle and never block a release.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-21)

**Merge** (`training/merge.py`; run by `orchestration.pipeline_dag.stage_merge` until 2026-10-23, and no longer called by the pipeline), implemented:
- refuses an adapter directory without `adapter_config.json` (a run's output root is not an adapter), and
  a base revision still `PIN_ME` when the base would come from the Hub;
- loads the base with `AutoModelForImageTextToText` in the merge dtype on **`device_map="cuda"`** — GPU
  only (`require_cuda`); every engine is released before merge runs — from the pod's local copy
  (`model.local_dir`) when present, applies the adapter with PEFT, `merge_and_unload()`, saves safetensors
  (5 GB shards) **and the processor** (vLLM reads the chat template and image-processor config from the
  model directory), then renames `.partial` into place;
- merges the checkpoint `checkpoint_eval` selected (`selected_checkpoint`), read from Blob on a resumed run;
  with nothing selected a real run refuses with a `PipelineError` rather than merging the output root. The
  same reader decides which adapter calibration, the gate and `package` use, so the adapter published is the
  one scored;
- `finetune` merged in bf16 and `all` took `--dtype` (`defaults.dtype`); the flag and the default are gone.

**Quantization plan** (`plan_quantization(..., scope=ADAPTER.name)`): the adapter's run quantizes its own
merged model into its own paths. No stage calls it since 2026-10-23 (the quantize stage and `--formats` are gone). The llm-compressor export
(`_export`) refuses without CUDA and is not wired yet — it raises `NotImplementedError` naming the recipe and
the ignore list — and needs its **own environment** (`requirements-quantize.txt`; PyPI name `llmcompressor`):
it requires `datasets>=4` where ms-swift 3 needs `<4`, and a transformers range vLLM 0.11 excludes.

**Package uploads real weights** (`orchestration.pipeline_dag._push_weights`, through
`artifact_registry/transfer.py`), after the release bundle and its routing check are built (IMPL-13 §4):
- the selected adapter, by `push_scoped_adapter`, to `adapters/scope/policy/v{n}/` — **only** `adapter_config.json` and `adapter_model.safetensors`
  (`ADAPTER_FILES`), never the checkpoint's optimizer and scheduler state (~1.5 GB of a rank-64 checkpoint).
  `finetune --push-adapters` publishes the same two files. That is the whole release since 2026-10-23: the
  merged model (`merged-models/scope/policy/v{n}/`) and the non-bf16 formats
  (`quantized-models/scope/policy/v{n}/vllm/{format}/`), pushed before, are no longer built.

The adapter push skips every file whose bytes Blob already holds, compared by the MD5
recorded with each upload (`BlobClient.upload_dir(..., skip_identical=True)`), so re-packaging a `gated` release (the only kind
`package` takes again) sends nothing; a blob that records no MD5 is sent again. A dry run still records where each would go. The run
manifest's `quantized_model` points at the first quantized format — never bf16, which is not an export.

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- Added the alignment line and dated *Current implementation* 2026-10-13.
- §1: the merge reads the selection through `selected_checkpoint` (process result, else `checkpoint_selection.json` in Blob); the staging output path is per scope.
- §2: `--formats bf16` (the default) makes quantize a no-op; `fp8_verified` has no CLI setter; the FP8 example notes it is refused until verified.
- §4: `validate_quant` judges supplied metrics only; no CLI supplies them yet, so the stage warns.
- *Current implementation*: the published adapter is `adapter_config.json` + `adapter_model.safetensors` only (also for `--push-adapters`); adapter and merged-model pushes skip identical files by MD5; the bundle and routing check come before any push; the llm-compressor export is not wired.
- Acceptance checklist: added the bf16 no-op and the two-file adapter publish.

## Changes since 2026-10-13

- Header: aligned with the code of 2026-10-21; architecture reference is `finetuning-architecture-v2.4.docx`.
- What changed from v1: GPU only (decision 2026-10-19); GGUF an optional edge export, never a deployment target.
- §2: the bf16 reference runs on any CUDA GPU, not any hardware.
- §2a: GGUF is not a deployment target; no command calls `export_gguf`, and the llama.cpp conversion is not wired.
- §5 (new): the stage order in `package`; the promotion gate's paired bootstrap, `--fixes-defect` and the interim cap for a small eval set; the push re-checks the decision; the bundle's tier; latency recorded, not gated (no SLA).
- Acceptance checklist: GPU only; the gate's place and the push's check; the bundle's tier and the small-set cap; latency never blocks.

## Changes of 2026-10-22 (code review)

- §5: the gate's bootstrap is weighted and pairs only reports scored on the same frozen set of the tenant; the interim cap follows what the scope was scored on; `--fixes-defect` per scope; the written override on the command line (IMPL-08).
- Acceptance checklist: the bundle's tier under the per-scope cap.
- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.5.docx` and the code of 2026-10-22.

## Changes of 2026-10-22 (one adapter)

- One adapter (decision 2026-10-22): merge, quantization and push paths are the adapter's (`.../scope/policy/v{n}/`); `package --version vN` with no `--release-id` (the release id is the version); the gate's baseline is the tenant's promoted release, `--fixes-defect` and the override are one value, and the interim cap counts real frozen policies; a passing release is promoted and the one it replaces archived.
- Code review of 2026-10-23: the gate scores the frozen policies of the lines the release serves; a dry run never promotes (its bundle stays `gated`); `package` refuses a version already promoted or archived, and a release with no trained line (§5, Current implementation).

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (incremental training)

- The pipeline no longer merges or quantizes (decision 2026-10-23): the release is the adapter, served as a LoRA on the bf16 base (`served_as: lora_on_base`); the `merge` and `quantize` stages, `--formats`, `--skip-quantize`, `--dtype` and the `formats`/`dtype` defaults are gone. Goal, §1, §2 and §4 mark `merge.py`, `quantize.py` and the threshold check as kept but not used; §5: `package` is calibrate → gate → push (stages 7–9), and the gate scores the base with the selected adapter applied; *Current implementation*: the adapter is the only push. Acceptance items updated.

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (architecture v2.6)

- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.6.docx`, which now records the one policy adapter, one version per cycle, incremental training and no merge or quantization.

## Changes of 2026-10-24 (architecture v2.7)

- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.7.docx`, which records the decisions of 2026-10-23 and 2026-10-24: printed values only, consistent output, fields first, one MinerU, production on Azure.

# IMPL-11_serving

# SPEC 11 — Serving (vLLM Endpoint + Classifier + Routing)

> Aligned with the code of 2026-10-23.

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-01, IMPL-02, IMPL-07 (inference core), IMPL-09 (calibration), IMPL-10 (postprocessing).
>
> **Naming hazard (master §0.1):** the architecture's "**Fideon SPEC_11**" is the external Presidio de-identification spec (implemented in our IMPL-05). This file is our serving layer. Different documents.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.7.docx` §0a (audit gate contract), §0b (LoB output), §0e (decisions of 2026-10-19: every page read), §4a (**classifier as a load-bearing component**), §4b (ACORD two-level), §4c (day-zero classifier), §5 (confidence), §7 (long documents: every page, read in windows), §14 (**RunPod split: what runs where**).

## Goal

Serve the promoted model on a RunPod Serverless vLLM endpoint with per-release adapter routing, document-type classification, windowed reading of every page of a long document, and calibrated confidence post-processing — built on the shared inference core so **serving == eval == testing**.

## The infrastructure boundary (arch §14) — get this right or the design leaks

```
Training (ephemeral pod)              Serving (persistent endpoint)
─────────────────────────             ─────────────────────────────
clone repo @ commit                   vLLM + promoted model
pull base + corpus (Blob)                     │
run LoRA training                     request ──> classify doc type
push adapter + manifest (Blob)                │
terminate                             select + hot-swap adapter
                                              │
External orchestrator                 inference + logprobs
triggers pods via RunPod API                  │
                                       calibrate confidence
Application logic (CPU infra,                 │
outside RunPod): OCR, prompt          return JSON + confidence
assembly, endpoint calls,
confidence calibration
```

**RunPod is scoped purely to GPU-bound work.** Application/orchestration logic — calling MinerU, assembling the prompt, calling the endpoint, applying the calibration transform, returning final JSON — runs in **your own service** (Azure Function, App Service, or AKS), not inside RunPod. This keeps business logic on cheap CPU infra, keeps it portable if the GPU provider changes, and keeps the RunPod handler small. Document this boundary in the module docstring; it is an architectural constraint, not a deployment preference.

## Deliverables

### 1. `serving/vllm_entrypoint.py`
- RunPod Serverless handler wrapping vLLM, serving the promoted release over an OpenAI-compatible API, **with logprobs enabled** (calibration depends on them). A release packaged since 2026-10-23 is its adapter (`served_as: lora_on_base`), served on the bf16 base; nothing is merged or quantized.
- **One LoRA per request, never a reload.** A tenant has one promoted release (decision 2026-10-22). A `lora_on_base` release always runs with the engine holding the **base with LoRA enabled** and the release's adapter applied per request (`serves_base_with_loras` is True for it; `load_release_runtimes`). An older merged release: when it covers every known line, the engine is that release's merged model and no adapter is applied; when it leaves lines to the base model, the engine loads the base with LoRA enabled and the release's adapter is applied per request. A §4.2 graduated per-type adapter would be applied the same way, from the classifier result. One LoRA per request is what vLLM supports, which is exactly why the v1 Foundation-plus-per-type stack could never have been served.
- Pulls the promoted release's adapter and calibrators from Blob on cold start (IMPL-02); version configurable. The model itself is handed to `cold_start` — loading it there is not wired yet, and `cold_start` refuses without one.
- Uses IMPL-07 `model_runner` / `input_builder` for generation so output matches eval and testing exactly.
- **Asserts the serving MinerU version matches the training corpus pin** (IMPL-03 `assert_version_matches`) wherever MinerU is installed — a mismatch is distribution shift and a regression trigger (arch §8a), not a warning to ignore. A serving pod without MinerU does no OCR; it logs the pin its requests' page texts must come from.
- **Every tuneable in `configs/inference/vllm_serving.yaml` must have a reader.** `serving_thresholds()` lifts `routing.classifier_confidence_threshold`, `confidence.review_threshold`, `routing.detect_lob`, `routing.lob_confidence_threshold`, `routing.lob_uncertain_threshold` and `routing.fallback_doc_type` out of the config and passes them into `extract`. (`routing.release_pins` and `release_pins()` are gone: a rollback is `promote --version vK`, IMPL-13. A config still holding `release_pins` or the removed `lob_family_threshold` is refused at cold start, `assert_no_retired_settings`.) (There is no page threshold to read: every page is read, in windows planned by the function the corpus build planned its training rows with, so nothing in serving's config can re-shape documents away from what was trained. The `long_documents` block keeps only `conflict_resolution: declarations_page_wins`, which names the rule the window merge applies by group order; no code reads it.) They were declared in that file and read by no code, so an operator could raise `review_threshold`, redeploy, and change nothing at all — the hardcoded defaults inside `extract` won silently every time. A test asserts each key the helper emits is a parameter `extract` accepts.
- **Calibration is selected after classification, not before.** The handler hands the whole `{doc_type: params}` map to `extract`, which picks the entry once the classifier has said what the document is. Resolving it in the handler from the caller-supplied `doc_type` meant a lookup on `""` for every request that did not name its own type — which is every classification-driven request, the endpoint's entire purpose — and the request was refused before extraction ran. `assert_calibration_present` accepts the map and checks it is non-empty; an empty one is still no calibration. It is checked only when no release is served, or some served release has no calibrators of its own.
- **The adapter map holds adapters that exist.** `resolve_model_version(..., doc_type=…)` constructs `type_adapter` unconditionally — it is a path, not evidence anything was trained — so `build_adapter_map` confirms the directory holds an `adapter_config.json` before mapping a doc type to it. Trusting the path mapped all three types on a `--foundation-only` build, and vLLM was then handed a `LoRARequest` for a directory that does not exist, failing every request instead of serving Foundation-only with a `routing:no_adapter_available` flag.
- **No plaintext PII in logs**; do not persist raw request/response bodies (master §8). The payload log call sits **inside** the handler's `try`: `safe_log_payload` iterates the payload, so an `input` that is not a JSON object raised straight out of the handler and RunPod returned an opaque platform error — the exact failure the structured-error contract exists to prevent.

### 2. `serving/doc_type_classifier.py`
**This is load-bearing, not preprocessing (arch §4a).** If classification is wrong, you load the wrong adapter *and* the wrong prompt *and* the wrong schema, and the extraction fails no matter how good the model is.

```
document ──> classifier ──> confidence >= threshold ? ──> yes ──> select adapter + prompt + schema
                                     │                              (acord -> + form number)
                                     └── no ──> Foundation-only extraction + human routing review
```

- Identifies doc type ∈ {`acord`, `policy`, `lossrun`} **and the ACORD form number** — the two-level classification of arch §4b. Returns label + confidence.
- Reads a document's **first two pages** — their images by page number and their text joined, cut at 4,000 characters (`classifier_input`, `classifier_messages`) — the same rule a training row is built by.
- **Default implementation: zero-shot via the Foundation model** (arch §4a Option C) using `doc_type_classifier_prompt.jinja` — zero extra infrastructure, and the Foundation already understands these document types.
- **Also reads a policy's line of business** when asked (`Classification.lob`), with the probability of the line's own tokens as its confidence (`line_confidence`) — never a number the model writes about itself. Used only with `routing.detect_lob` on, which ships **off** (§6, *Current implementation*). With detection on and no classifier supplied, the endpoint builds `ZeroShotClassifier` on the engine's base weights with no adapter (`line_classifier`) — only when the engine holds the base; an engine holding one release's merged model reads no line. The line decides whether the release's adapter or the base model reads the policy, so no adapter can have been chosen yet.
- Interface is swappable for a **dedicated vision classifier** (Option B) later, which is the right escalation if classification accuracy becomes a *measured* bottleneck — it works in both OCR and image-only modes, unlike a text-feature classifier.
- **Day-zero (arch §4c):** before Foundation v1.0 exists, the classifier runs against the **base** `Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct`. This path is explicitly temporary and is superseded on promotion of Foundation v1.0, after which the base model is retired from the production inference path.
- **Has its own accuracy target and is measured on the golden eval set** alongside extraction metrics (IMPL-08 `classifier_accuracy`) — a classifier at 92% caps the whole system at 92%.
- A request that names its `doc_type` (and `acord_form`) skips classification (`StaticClassifier`); with no classifier held and detection off, a request without `doc_type` has nothing to classify it.

### 3. `serving/adapter_router.py`
- Maps classifier result → (adapter path, prompt file, schema). Deterministic selection.
- **Low-confidence fallback (arch §4a):** when the classifier isn't confident, **don't silently guess**. Fall back to **Foundation-only extraction** with a generic schema-agnostic prompt and flag the document for human routing review. A wrong-adapter extraction is worse than a slightly-generic one.
- Runs **before** adapter selection in both the serving path and the testing routine.
- For ACORD, resolves the form number to the matching schema even when a single shared `acord` adapter is in use (arch §4b Option 1) — the classifier must identify the specific form so the correct schema is always selected.

### 4. `serving/page_router.py`
Long-document handling, primarily a **Policy-document** concern (arch §7). Policy documents can run 50+ pages; each page at full resolution costs 1,000–2,000+ vision tokens, so a whole policy cannot go into one prompt. **Every page is read, chunk by chunk, never selected pages** (decision 2026-10-19, arch §0e). Page selection was removed: a page that scored nothing was never read, at training or at serving, and whatever it printed was silently missing.

1. **Declarations page** — `plan_pages` returns **every page**, in order, and the first page whose MinerU text matches a declarations signal (`declarations_page`). The other `PAGE_SIGNALS` (coverage schedule and coverage summary 6; schedule 5; named insured, policy number, policy period 4; premium, deductible, limits of insurance 3; endorsement, producer 2) stay on the plan's scores for tracing and decide nothing: detecting a page labels it and drops nothing. There is no page threshold, no selection threshold and no `RoutingPlan.routed`. `data_pipeline.dataset_builder.policy_windows.pages_to_read` wraps it — `(every page, declarations page)` — so training and serving plan by one rule; with no OCR text (`image_only`) every page is read and no declarations page is detected.
2. **Windowed extraction (policies)** — every policy (every policy's output is canonical) is read as **section group × page window**, planned by `policy_windows.plan_windows` over every page, the same function the corpus build expanded its training rows with. **Every group reads every page** (`common.schema_sections.pages_for`; the page rules in `configs/schema_sections.yaml` are `every` and `declarations`). `decl` (and `dtd` where a line carries it) takes as its first window pages 1–5 (`pages.declarations_leading: 5`) plus the detected declarations page, read together (`leading_pages`); the pages after them follow in windows, as every other group's do. Windows hold `pages_per_extraction_call` pages, with split runs overlapping by `run_overlap_pages` (1 page on the common model), and a window whose pages' own OCR text would not fit its call is split into windows that do (`expand_tasks.fit_windows_to_text`, the rule the corpus build sizes its rows with; `image_only` windows by page count alone). Each window's prompt and decoding schema are its group's slice. More windows per policy than under page selection, so a longer read. See §6.
3. **Other types** — there is no routed single call. A Loss Run is windowed only when its estimated rows overflow one answer; anything else (an ACORD form, a Loss Run that fits) is read in one call over every page, interleaved `[img_1][<page 1 of N> + md_1][img_2][<page 2 of N> + md_2]…` (IMPL-07). One call per page was the earlier design and was wrong twice over: it asked the model to produce a complete document-level JSON from one page — a shape no training row ever had — and it prevented the model from seeing that a table on page 9 continues on page 10. Interleaving makes a policy window a **subsequence of the full document**, so the served shape is the trained shape with fewer pairs.
4. **Merge** — a policy's windows are merged by `serving/policy_merge.merge_policy_windows`, a long Loss Run's by `serving/lossrun_merge.merge_extracted_windows` (§6). `merge_page_extractions` (the declarations-page-wins rule for per-page results) stays in `page_router.py` as code only; it is not on the serving path, and the declarations rule is stated to the model in the Policy prompt and applied by the window merge's group order.

The page numbers in the markers are the document's real numbers, so a window's numbering tells the model it is holding a fragment: fields living on pages it was not shown are absent, not missing. `image_only` carries the markers without markdown — the guarantee is *no OCR text*, not *no text at all*.

- **No document is page-routed** — a policy of any length is read over every page, in windows; there is no list of routed types (`long_doc_types` is gone).
- **Record which pages fed each extraction** in the output metadata (`pages_used`, master §9), so a low-confidence field can be traced to the specific page it came from. A policy's `pages_used` is every page.

### 5. Confidence post-processing — composed in `serving/pipeline.py`
*(`calibration/apply_calibration.py` + `calibration/list_completeness.py`, called from the pipeline, rather than a `serving/confidence_postprocess.py` wrapper. The steps below are all implemented; a serving-side wrapper around two calibration modules would be a second place for the output contract to be shaped, and the contract has to have exactly one.)*
- Applies IMPL-09 calibration to raw logprob confidence (the release's per-field-type calibrators and thresholds when it has them; otherwise the v1 transform, with every field flagged `:uncalibrated`), adds the **list-completeness** signal, shapes the final output contract (master §9), and flags sub-threshold fields for review.
- Emits `line_of_business`: for a policy, the line resolution `{lines, source, confidence, candidates, hypothesis_agreed}` (`source` ∈ `caller`, `detected`, `detected_uncertain`, `undetected`); otherwise the standard `{value, confidence}` shape (arch §0b) collapsed from the line spans, `null` when undetermined.
- **Validates the final JSON against the doc_type schema before returning** — this mirrors the Fideon SPEC_07 Stage 3 audit gate (arch §0a). A schema-invalid response is an error, not a returned result. On a common-model line the served JSON (every key filled) is what is judged, and a required section no window wrote, or an object written empty (`minProperties`), still fails. The judgement is one function, `serving.pipeline.schema_verdict` (a `SchemaVerdict`: the answer as judged, the filled answer, `valid`, `errors`), shared with evaluation: `served_schema_verdict` envelopes a merged common-model answer and fills its system fields first, then judges it the same way, so checkpoint selection (`evaluation/metrics/coverage_metrics.score_schema_validity`) judges a whole common-model document as serving would return it. Checked as written, the merged answer lacked what the pipeline adds and `document_schema_validity_rate` read a false 0%.
- **Attaches the full-text tier** on a common-model policy (`common.full_text.build_text_sections`): `text_sections` — every printed word, by form and section — built from the request's page text by Fideon's deterministic section builder (`common/section_builder`, FideonSLM's `sections.py` and `pdf_text.py` copied byte for byte, recorded in `SOURCE.json`; `scripts/check_section_builder.py` reports drift), never by the model (SPEC_21 §4.6). Before validation, so the client's schema judges it. A request with no page text (image-only) gets none and is flagged `text_sections:no_page_text`. Each result carries `text_coverage`, the share of the page text's words found in the served JSON (SPEC_21's every-word rule, check C4; `None` with no page text). Our wrapper anchors each MinerU table so the builder writes it: the copied builder drops a markdown table otherwise (to be fixed in FideonSLM).
- **Fills every key of the line's schema** (`common.canonical.with_all_keys`, without the client's `fideon:` annotation properties) after validation and confidence: a value not extracted is `null`, not absent, so every served policy JSON — base model or trained — has the same keys. A post-process, never trained.
- Never returns raw (uncalibrated) confidence as if calibrated — IMPL-09 raises when calibration is missing.

### 6. `serving/pipeline.py` (request orchestrator)
Per request: classify (or take the caller's `doc_type`) → route adapter/prompt/schema → resolve the policy's line (caller's; detected only with `detect_lob` on) → route through the serving plan (the promoted release, or base model + `_fallback.json`) → read: policy windows / Loss Run windows / one call → merge → serving guards (common-model lines) → printed values only (every policy) → IMPL-09 confidence → list completeness → canonical envelope → schema validation → every-key fill → final JSON.

**This is the canonical pipeline the testing harness (IMPL-12) reuses**, which is what guarantees test == prod. Testing must call it, never re-implement it. The golden eval (IMPL-08) reads through it too, and checkpoint selection's whole-document pass merges its windows with the same `serving/policy_merge.py`.

## Constraints
- Non-GPU orchestration runs **outside** RunPod; the RunPod handler stays scoped to inference (arch §14). Document the boundary.
- Deterministic adapter/prompt/schema selection.
- Calibrated (never raw) confidence in responses.
- Every response schema-validated before return.
- MinerU version parity with the training corpus asserted.
- No PII in logs; no persisted raw request/response bodies.
- Line-of-business detection stays off (`routing.detect_lob: false`) until it has been measured.

## Acceptance checklist
- [ ] Endpoint serves a request end-to-end → schema-shaped JSON + calibrated confidence + `line_of_business`.
- [ ] Where a type has graduated, the correct per-type adapter is hot-swapped from the classification result; otherwise the release serves it — its adapter on the base, or an older release's merged model with no adapter — and that is NOT flagged for review — flagging the expected path would queue every document.
- [ ] ACORD classification returns both `doc_type` and `acord_form`, and the form selects the schema.
- [ ] Low classifier confidence → **Foundation-only fallback + review flag**, never a silent guess.
- [ ] A policy of any length is read in windows over **every page**, and its `pages_used` is every page; a page no section signal matches is read like any other.
- [ ] The declarations group's (and `dtd`'s) first window is pages 1–5 plus the detected declarations page, read together; a declarations page behind a cover sheet joins it; every later page follows in windows.
- [ ] A window's user turn is a **subsequence** of the full document's — the same blocks, fewer pairs. Comparing system prompts alone does not catch a divergence here.
- [ ] `pages_used` reflects the pages actually sent, including for `image_only` documents that carry no per-page OCR text.
- [ ] Nothing in serving's config or a release selects pages: there is no page threshold and no `long_doc_types`.
- [ ] A window that fails (unparseable, or cut at the token limit) is split in half and retried; one that still fails alone is dropped and flagged `window_failed:<group>:p<page>`, and the rest of the document is returned.
- [ ] A served policy JSON carries every key of its line's schema, `null` where nothing was extracted.
- [ ] In strict mode a value no page prints is taken out of a served policy JSON (its key null; an extra field, or a row with nothing printed, leaves its list, the rows after it renumbered) and flagged `<path>:not_printed_removed`; flag mode keeps and flags it `<path>:not_printed`; an unknown mode is refused; with no page text nothing is checked and the result is flagged `printed_values:no_page_text`.
- [ ] A served common-model policy with page text carries `text_sections` from the section builder, valid against the client's schema, with every word of its page text somewhere in the JSON (`text_coverage` 1.0 on the test fixtures); one with none is flagged `text_sections:no_page_text`. The copied builder matches `SOURCE.json`.
- [ ] A policy with no line, detection off, is refused unless the caller sends `allow_lob_fallback`; a known line the promoted release did not train on, and a package even of lines it trained on, is read by the base model against `_fallback.json`, flagged `route:lob_fallback_used`.
- [ ] One promoted release serves a tenant; if the index holds several, the newest that can be served serves, with a warning naming it. Cold start reads the release's `window_plan_version` (`ServedRelease`), and refuses a config still holding `routing.release_pins` or `routing.lob_family_threshold`. It also refuses a `lora_on_base` release whose adapter trained on another base than the configured `model_id@revision`; an older merged release is not checked.
- [ ] A schema-invalid generation is rejected, not returned.
- [ ] Checkpoint selection judges a merged common-model answer with serving's verdict (`served_schema_verdict`), not a stricter copy of it.
- [ ] On a common-model line, a form's edition left in its form number in an unambiguous shape is moved to the form's empty `edition_date` (`MM/YY`) and takes the number's span; a filled `edition_date` is never overwritten.
- [ ] Missing calibration for the served version raises rather than returning raw confidence.
- [ ] MinerU version mismatch against the corpus pin fails loudly.
- [ ] No PII in logs; no raw bodies persisted.
- [ ] `serving/pipeline.py` is the single path reused by IMPL-12.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-21)

**Canonical policies are read as windows over every page** (`serving/pipeline.py::_extract_policy_windows`):
every section group × page window planned by `data_pipeline/dataset_builder/policy_windows.plan_windows`
over every page (`pages_to_read`: every page, and the declarations page `serving.page_router.plan_pages`
finds in the same text the training rows were planned on), whatever the document's length. The routed
single-call branch, the page threshold and `long_doc_types` are gone from `extract`. Each round's windows
go to vLLM in **calls of at most 96** (`MAX_WINDOWS_PER_BATCH`, the validation's `validation_batch_rows`; not yet measured in serving; `generate_batch`), the longest expected answers first (`_send_order`: the larger answer reservation, then more pages; `ExtractionRequest.send_order="reversed"` sends them the other way, as the golden eval's repeat pass does) and read back in planned order, so a
69-page policy's 81 windows are one call and a 200-page policy's page images are not all decoded at once. vLLM fails every prompt of a call when it refuses one, so a call whose windows all failed with one error is read again in halves (`_batch_in_halves`) until the window that caused it is alone, and only that window fails. Each window's answer limit is its section task's reserved budget
(`common.config.answer_cap(task_for(group))`), its page references are bounded to its own pages
(`common.schemas.with_page_bounds`), and an answer cut at the token limit is refused even if it parses. A
window that fails is **split in half and retried** in the next round (decoding is greedy, so an unchanged
retry would write the same thing); one that still fails at a single page is dropped and flagged
`window_failed:<group>:p<page>`. Only when no window succeeds is the request an error. `window_latencies_ms`
holds one entry per batch (and per window read alone after a whole-batch failure). This is how **200+ page
policies** are served. At cold start `assert_window_plan_served` refuses a release serving policies whose
`window_plan_version` is not this code's `window_plan_id()` — one that records none was packaged before every
page was read.

**The window merge** (`serving/policy_merge.py::merge_policy_windows`):
- *Self-contained lines* (the frozen non-SPEC_21 schemas): windows merged in group order then page order, so
  `decl` wins a conflict; a later differing value is kept out and flagged `<path>:merge_conflict`; arrays
  concatenate and are de-duplicated on the `configs/schema_sections.yaml` `array_keys`, a duplicate keeping
  the more complete row and the union of page references.
- *Common-model lines* (the seven personal lines, `gl`, and the fallback — no line): each window's ids are
  namespaced; **units** (locations, buildings, vehicles, drivers, watercraft, scheduled items, LOB parts) are
  joined on their own keys (`unit_keys`: VIN, location number, ...) and every reference rewritten to the
  merged unit; a row read twice because two windows share an overlap page is dropped from one of them — never
  a unit's row (`_drop_shared_page_rows`); the other tables are joined on the line's keys with references
  compared through the units they name, and a row whose reference its window could not see joins the one row
  it matches (`_join_unreferenced`); the whole document is then renumbered in printed order
  (`common.structural_ids.renumber_structural_ids`, the numbering training uses). Flags:
  `:joined_without_identifier`, `:ambiguous_unit`, `:unplaced_fragment`, `:dangling_reference`.

**Serving guards on common-model lines** (`serving/conventions.py`, after the merge):
- `conform` applies the label rules (`data_pipeline.ingestion.label_rules.apply_label_rules`) against every
  page's text: each value once, citing every page that prints it; an extra field moved into its own empty
  field. A value no page prints is flagged `<path>:not_printed`; on a born-digital document
  (`ocr_meta.modality` native) an extra field no page prints is dropped, flagged
  `additional_fields:not_printed_dropped`. A scan's OCR text settles nothing for the label rules, so here a
  scan's values all stay; the printed-values check below judges scans too, and in strict mode its
  `not_printed_removed` replaces `not_printed`.
- `conform` also moves a form's edition left inside its form number to the form's empty `edition_date`, as the
  labels now do (`label_rules.split_form_edition`): only an unambiguous shape — ISO's `AA 99 99 MM YY`, or a
  month and year in brackets after the number — parsed `MM/YY`, the number kept without it; a filled
  `edition_date` is never overwritten. The moved edition takes the number's span, since it was read in the
  number's tokens.
- `likely_missed` flags `<block>.<field>:likely_missed` where a declarations field was left empty while a line
  of any page opens with its label and a value of its type (`_likely_missed` passes every page: the
  declarations windows read every page).
- `unit_link_flags` flags `coverages[i].applies_to:unit_link_unknown` for a coverage served without its unit
  when the answer holds two or more vehicles, boats or buildings, or another row of its code does carry a link.
- Empty optional objects and empty rows are dropped from the answer (`_without_empty`).

**Printed values only** (`serving/printed_values.py`, decision 2026-10-24). After the merge and `conform`,
every canonical policy's values are looked up on the document's pages with
`common.grounding.ground_tolerant` (IMPL-08 §1), on scans too: each envelope with a non-empty `raw`, extra
fields and table rows included; system fields are skipped.
- Printed on some page: kept. Unchecked (too short, a yes/no reading, a cited page with no text): kept and
  counted.
- Printed nowhere, `strict` mode (the default; `printed_values.mode` in `configs/inference/vllm_serving.yaml`,
  read by `configured_mode`, which refuses an unknown mode): taken out of the JSON. Its key stays, null on a
  common-model line; on a self-contained line it is left out, and the every-key fill serves it as null
  (`hold_to_printed(..., lob=)`). An extra field leaves its list. A table row goes when nothing in it is
  found printed (a value too short to check keeps it); the rows after it move up, and the structural ids are
  numbered again as the merge numbers them. Spans go, or move, with their values. Each value is flagged
  `<path>:not_printed_removed`, on the path it had in the model's answer; that flag replaces `conform`'s
  `<path>:not_printed`, and other flags follow their values or go with them.
- Printed nowhere, `flag` mode: kept and flagged `<path>:not_printed`.

The record is `ExtractionResult.printed_check`: `{mode, checked, unchecked, not_printed: [{path, raw, pages}],
removed}`, `not_printed` listed before removal. It is not in `as_dict()`; the golden eval reads it (IMPL-08).
With no page text (image only) nothing is checked: `printed_check` is None and the result is flagged
`printed_values:no_page_text`. Fields first serves its fields only once held to the pages they cite
(`serving.fields_first.verify_fields`: `hold_result_to_printed` on a copy, over those pages' text; a value
not found yet is held back, `pending_full_text`), and checks the whole result once MinerU's full text arrives
(`attach_full_text` → `hold_result_to_printed`): for every policy, before the full-text tier, only when no
check has run yet; `fields`, `list_fields`, `overall_confidence`, the flags and the schema verdict follow
the JSON. The trade-off the user accepted: a value MinerU misread beyond what the search forgives is removed
too, and comes back null with the flag.

**Long Loss Runs** (`lossrun_windows`, `_extract_lossrun_windows`): windowed only when the estimated rows of
the whole document overflow one answer (`answer_cap("lossrun_rows")`, 8,192 tokens), with the same split-and-
retry rule; merged by `serving/lossrun_merge.merge_extracted_windows` and reconciled against the printed totals.
Flags `claims:merge_conflict`, `claims:totals_mismatch`; the output carries `reconciliation`.

**The request** (`serving/vllm_entrypoint.py::build_request`):
- `lob` (or `line_of_business`): one line, or a list for a package policy, normalised to lower case with classic
  auto read as `personal_auto` (`common.lob.merge_line`). It selects the line's canonical schema; a single line
  with no schema is **refused** unless the caller sends `allow_lob_fallback`.
- `lob_hypothesis`: a line L1/L2 suspects without being sure; reconciled with the line the classifier reads.
- `source_file_name` (or `file_name`): written into the canonical output by the system, never asked of the model.
- `page_texts` — MinerU's per-page markdown, from MinerU 3.4.5's pipeline backend at the corpus pin — must number
  exactly pages `1..n` against the `n` images sent, or the request is refused; a joined `ocr_text` is accepted for
  a single page only. Images are paired **by page number** (`page_N.` by file name, else position); a page beyond
  the images is an error. `ocr_meta` carries the document's modality.

**Line of business** (`serving/pipeline.py::_resolve_lob`): the caller's line wins (flagged
`lob:caller_disagrees` when a confident classifier reads another). **Detection ships off**
(`routing.detect_lob: false`) until `scripts/measure_lob_detection.py` has measured it on held-out policies; off,
a policy with no line is refused, or read against the fallback when the caller sends `allow_lob_fallback`. On: a
line read at or above `lob_confidence_threshold` (0.90) routes as the caller's would; at or above
`lob_uncertain_threshold` (0.60, was `lob_family_threshold`) it is read with that line's schema, flagged
`lob:uncertain` (was `lob:uncertain_within_family`) for a person to check; below, the base model reads the
policy against the fallback, flagged `lob:undetected`. The LOB detection report no longer has
`family_accuracy`.

**One promoted release per tenant** (`serving/release_router.py`, `build_serving_plan`, `ServingPlan.route`;
decision 2026-10-22). The release marked `promoted` in the tenant's release index serves; should the index
hold several, the newest that can be served serves and the warning names it. There are no layout families
(`configs/layout_families.yaml` is gone; Fideon SPEC_06 §9a / SPEC_09's family adapters are knowingly not
followed). The release records the lines it trained on (`lines`: the single lines of the run's train view,
else the corpus's `policy_line_counts`; never none for a new release, since packaging refuses one, IMPL-13):
- a policy of ONE line the release covers goes to it (`ServedRelease.covers_lob`, `common.lob.single_line`);
- any other known line, and a package even of lines the release covers (it is read against `_fallback.json`,
  a shape the adapter is not taught) — every line, while nothing is promoted — is read by the **base model
  with no LoRA against `_fallback.json`**, flagged `route:lob_fallback_used`, with the release's calibrators
  dropped (every field goes to review). Before the code review of 2026-10-23 a package of self-contained
  lines went to the release. It is refused when the engine holds an older release's merged model rather than
  the base (`base_model_loaded`; a `lora_on_base` engine always holds the base); the error says a policy with no line, a package or an undetected line cannot be
  read there, and to send the policy's single line;
- a policy with no line, or a line with no schema, is refused unless the caller sends `allow_lob_fallback` (or
  detection found no line); an older promoted release that records no lines reads it instead;
- a document of a type the release does not cover is refused (`UnservedDocType`).
