`route` in the output records `lob_fallback_used` and `lob_source` (no `layout_family`). The release step
records the same routing as a table on the bundle and fails when a line cannot be routed, or a line the
release trained on would not reach it; it routes every known line, the canonical policy schemas
(`serving/routing_check.py`, IMPL-13 §4).

**Serving through the release** (`serving/vllm_entrypoint.load_release_runtimes`, `extract(...,
release_runtimes=)`): cold start builds the release's adapter and its fitted calibrators and
thresholds (`releases/.../calibration/{format}/calibrators.json`). A `lora_on_base` release (every release
since 2026-10-23) always has the engine load the base with LoRA enabled (`serves_base_with_loras` is True for
it): its adapter (`ServedRelease.adapter_dir`, the bundle's `serving_formats.bf16`) is downloaded to
`staging/serving-adapters/<release_id>/` and applied per request. For an older merged release, when every
known line reaches it the engine serves its merged model and no adapter is applied; when it leaves a known
line to the base model, the engine loads the base with LoRA enabled and applies the release's adapter the same
way — cold start refuses a model loaded without LoRA support (`enable_lora: false`), or a release whose
adapter is missing. `extract` routes each request through the release `route` returned. The v1 per-version
calibration is needed only for a release without its own calibrators. A rollback is a promotion
(`promote --version vK`, IMPL-13), not a pin. Cold start does not require a unified run when a release is
promoted. **Fixed (2026-10-22):** `ServedRelease` now carries `window_plan_version`, which
`assert_window_plan_served` reads at cold start; without it, any promoted policy release raised
`AttributeError`. No release lists page-routed types any more: `long_doc_types` and `long_doc_types_for` were
removed, and every served type reads every page.

**Parity with training**: the same blank-page placeholder, the same pixel budget (engine
`mm_processor_kwargs`), the same window plan over every page (`pages_to_read`, `plan_windows`), dates in `MM/DD/YYYY` by the same word rule,
the same classifier input, and the calibration page text by the same definition
(`calibration.features.shown_ocr_text`).

**Cold start** (`assert_prompt_hash`): each release records the hash of **every prompt input** — all
templates (`prompts/**/*.jinja`, the per-doc-type ones included), every schema (ours and the client's
canonical files) and the section map. A release packaged with different prompt inputs is **refused**; one
with no recorded hash is warned about. A release served as a LoRA on the base (`lora_on_base`) is also
refused when its adapter trained on another base than the configured `model_id@revision`
(`configs/base_model.yaml`; `assert_base_model_served`, through `query_registry.base_model_refusal`): a
moved revision pin changes the weights under the adapter, which would still load and answer with no error.
An older merged release carries its own base and is not checked. `promote` runs the same prompt-hash,
window-plan and base-model checks (`registry_utils.releases.serving_refusal`), so a release this endpoint
would refuse is never promoted (IMPL-13). Cold start also refuses a serving config still holding a removed setting, `routing.release_pins`
or `routing.lob_family_threshold` (`vllm_entrypoint.assert_no_retired_settings`): read by nothing, it would be
ignored without a word.

**Engine**: vLLM `==0.11.0` (the build calibration was fitted with); refuses to start without CUDA; loads the
base from the pod's local copy (for every `lora_on_base` release), or an older release's merged model.

**OCR pin at cold start**: the corpus manifest must still record `mineru_version`, but the version comparison
runs only where MinerU is installed. A serving pod does no OCR (requests carry page texts produced by the pinned
MinerU), and comparing against a MinerU it does not have refused every deployment. A real mismatch is a
`ColdStartError`.

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- Added the alignment line and dated *Current implementation* 2026-10-13.
- §1: base + LoRA per request when several releases are promoted or a release leaves lines to the base model; `serving_thresholds()` also reads `detect_lob`, the two line thresholds and `fallback_doc_type`; `release_pins()`; the model is supplied to `cold_start`.
- §2: the classifier's two-page input; line-of-business reading with token-probability confidence, used only with detection on (ships off); the base-model line classifier.
- §4: replaced "one call for the selected pages, no merge" for policies with routed-page windows (`plan_windows`: decl over 5 leading pages + declarations page, arrays/lineblk over routed pages, 1-page overlap); documented the page signals, the ≤5-page rule and that short documents are still scored; the single interleaved call is now the path for other long types; merges are `policy_merge` / `lossrun_merge`.
- §5/§6: line-resolution output shape; validation of the filled answer on common-model lines; every-key post-process; updated request flow.
- *Current implementation*: window batching, answer caps, page bounds, split-and-retry, `window_failed`; common-model merge (units, joins, overlap duplicates, renumbering) and its flags; serving guards (`not_printed`, extra-field drop on born-digital, `likely_missed`, `unit_link_unknown`, empty-object drop); Loss Run windows; new request keys (`lob_hypothesis`, `allow_lob_fallback`, `source_file_name`, classic auto → personal auto, MinerU 3.4.5 page texts); line resolution with detection off; routing by layout family with the base-model fallback (replacing "refused"); the release routing table.
- Acceptance checklist: replaced the single-call policy item; added window retry, every-key output and fallback routing items.

## Changes since 2026-10-13

- Header and goal: aligned with the code of 2026-10-21; architecture reference `finetuning-architecture-v2.4.docx` (§0e added); "page routing" replaced by windowed reading of every page.
- §1: no page threshold to read (`DEFAULT_LONG_DOC_PAGE_THRESHOLD` is gone); `long_documents` keeps only `conflict_resolution`, read by no code.
- §4: rewritten for every page read (decision 2026-10-19): `plan_pages` returns every page and only detects the declarations page; `pages_to_read` replaces `routed_pages`; every group reads every page, the declarations group's (and `dtd`'s) first window pages 1–5 plus the declarations page; the routed single-call branch, `SELECTION_THRESHOLD`, `RoutingPlan.routed`, the page cap and `long_doc_types` removed.
- §5: the schema judgement is `schema_verdict`, shared with evaluation through `served_schema_verdict`.
- Acceptance checklist: every-page and declarations-window items replace the routed-page and page-cap items; new items for the served schema verdict and form editions.
- *Current implementation*: windows over every page; `conform` moves a form's edition to `edition_date` with the number's span; `likely_missed` reads every page; `long_doc_types_for` removed; parity names the window plan, not a page threshold.

## Changes of 2026-10-22 (code review)

- Windows split by their pages' own OCR text, by the rule the corpus build uses (`fit_windows_to_text`); `image_only` windows by page count alone.
- A round's windows go to vLLM in batches of at most 32 (`MAX_WINDOWS_PER_BATCH`, chosen, not measured), one latency entry per batch.
- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.5.docx` and the code of 2026-10-22.
- A batch whose windows all failed with one error is read again window by window; latency is one entry per batch.
- Cold start refuses a policy release whose recorded window plan is not this code's (`assert_window_plan_served`).

## Changes of 2026-10-22 (one adapter)

- One promoted release per tenant (decision 2026-10-22): no layout families and no `release_pins` (a rollback is `promote`); a policy of a line the release trained on goes to it, any other known line to the base model against `_fallback.json`; `route` has no `layout_family`; the routing check routes every known line; `lob_uncertain_threshold` and `lob:uncertain` replace the family names, and the LOB detection report drops `family_accuracy`; fixed: `ServedRelease` carries `window_plan_version`, which the cold-start check reads. New acceptance item.
- Code review of 2026-10-23: `covers_lob` covers a policy of ONE line the release trained on, so a package of trained lines now goes to the base model against `_fallback.json`, flagged `lob_fallback_used`; cold start refuses a config still holding `routing.release_pins` or `routing.lob_family_threshold` (`assert_no_retired_settings`); `promote` runs the cold-start prompt-hash and window-plan checks; the several-promoted warning names the release served; the merged-engine error no longer suggests a remedy that does not exist. Acceptance items updated.

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (window calls, forms job, full text)

- A round's windows go to vLLM in calls of at most 96 (`MAX_WINDOWS_PER_BATCH`, was 32): at 32 a call, about one call in four of the CGL checkpoint selection waited ~140 s on one long window with the GPU nearly idle. A 69-page policy (81 windows) is one call. Not yet measured in serving.
- Sent longest expected answer first (`_send_order`), read back in planned order: the windows, the prompts and what the merge is given are unchanged.
- A call refused whole is read again in halves (`_batch_in_halves`), not window by window; latency is one entry per call.
- The forms list is read by a `forms` group of its own on the common-model lines (configs/schema_sections.yaml): the same windows as before on the personal lines, one more group (the claims-made terms and countersignature) on general liability; merged after `arrays`.
- The full-text tier is served (decision 2026-10-23): `text_sections` from the page text by Fideon's section builder (`common/full_text.py` over `common/section_builder`, FideonSLM 3d40d77 copied byte for byte), before validation; `text_sections:no_page_text` without page text; `text_coverage` on every common-model result. The copied builder dropped every MinerU table; the wrapper anchors them.

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (served model speed)

- A backend that answers each request on its own (`ModelBackend.supports_submit`; the served model, `inference_core/openai_backend.py`) has a policy's windows read as they are answered (`_read_as_answered`): every window sent at once, longest first, and a failed window's halves sent the moment it fails rather than after the slowest window of its round. The answers are merged in the rounds' order (by round, then planned position), so the merge is the same whichever window finished first; the in-process engine still reads in rounds of calls (`_read_in_rounds`). One `window_latencies_ms` entry for the whole reading.
- A streamed answer whose last 3,000 characters are one passage of at most 500 repeated (`repeating_tail`) is stopped and its connection closed, finish reason `repetition` (`STOPPED_REPEATING`), which counts as cut off: the window is split as at the token limit, instead of writing to 12,288 tokens.
- Every window read is recorded in `ExtractionResult.window_stats` (group, pages, attempt, outcome, seconds, time to first token, prompt and answer tokens, finish reason), not in the served JSON; `Generation` carries `prompt_tokens` and `first_token_ms`.
- Page images can go to a served model by path (`images="file"`, a `file://` URL; vLLM needs `--allowed-local-media-path`) instead of as base64.

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (page bytes)

- A served model is sent a gray page (an RGB render whose channels are equal everywhere) as an 8-bit grayscale PNG (`inference_core.page_links.sent_png`, `OpenAIChatBackend(gray_pages=True)`, the default): vLLM converts every image to RGB, so the model reads the same pixels, at 66% of the bytes (all 114 pages of a scan checked); a page with any colour, or one PIL cannot read, goes as it is on disk. Each page is encoded once however many windows send it.
- `images="link"` (`inference_core.page_links.PageLinks`): each page is uploaded once to Blob (`transient/page-links/<run>/<document>/`) and every window's request carries a read-only link the server fetches itself, instead of the image about four times a page; a page with no link goes inline. The link's token is a read-only SAS given (`PAGE_LINK_SAS_TOKEN`, refused unless `sp=r`) or one signed for the blob alone from the account key (`BlobClient.read_link`, IMPL-02). The pages are deleted after the document; the links expire (1 hour when signed here).

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (incremental training)

- Nothing is merged (decision 2026-10-23): a release is its adapter (`served_as: lora_on_base`), so the engine always holds the bf16 base and applies the release's adapter per request (`serves_base_with_loras` is True for it); an older merged release is served as before (§1, acceptance, *Current implementation*: serving through the release, the engine).
- Code review of the incremental training: cold start refuses a `lora_on_base` release whose adapter trained on another base than the configured one (`assert_base_model_served`), and `promote` runs the same check (*Current implementation*, cold start; acceptance).

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (architecture v2.6)

- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.6.docx`, which now records the one policy adapter, one version per cycle, incremental training and no merge or quantization.

## Changes of 2026-10-24 (production on Azure)

- The routine runs on Azure and RunPod serves only a stock vLLM and the MinerU 4 API (no code of ours there). Every served document's text comes from the pinned MinerU (`configs/shared/ocr.yaml`, IMPL-03): `ServedOcr(expected_version=)` refuses a task another version or tier read.
- `OpenAIChatBackend(model=name)` checks at start that the server lists `name` and, for a LoRA, that its `parent` is our base model (`configs/base_model.yaml`); a server that cannot list its models is not checked. `scripts/served_adapter.py` makes the promoted release's adapter ready for the pod's network volume: it refuses a release not served as `lora_on_base`, one whose OCR pin is not the served MinerU's, and an adapter vLLM cannot serve (LoRA on the vision side, `modules_to_save`, a rank above 512), downloads it as `policy-<version>`, and prints the copy command and the vLLM flags (`--enable-lora --max-lora-rank --max-loras 1 --lora-modules`).
- Model and MinerU URLs must be HTTPS, or HTTP to this machine only (`common.transport.assert_encrypted`); page links expire after an hour. Outside the code: the Azure region near the RunPod data centre, RunPod Secure Cloud under a data processing agreement, vLLM's request logging off.

## Changes of 2026-10-24 (fields first)

- One H100 serves both the model and MinerU, and the gate is a 200-page scan in 2 minutes (decision 2026-10-24); MinerU alone takes 4-5 minutes for it. `serving/fields_first.py` reads a document in two phases: `read_fields` renders the pages and the model reads their images (`image_only`) - the fields, served flagged `text_sections:no_page_text` - and `add_full_text` has MinerU read the PDF afterwards and adds the full-text tier (`serving.pipeline.attach_full_text`: Fideon's section builder over the page text, the result checked against the client's schema with it, `text_coverage` measured; a tier that fails the schema is left out, flagged `text_sections:invalid`). Only a result flagged `no_page_text` gets one: `extract` flags exactly the documents that have the tier and were read without text.
- `GpuTurns` orders the one GPU: MinerU starts a run of pages only while no document's fields are being read (`ocr_via_api(gate=)`: the runs then read one at a time, each to its end), so a document arriving while another's text is read waits at most one run.
- Whether image-only fields are accurate enough is measured before relying on them (IMPL-12, `latency_check --runs standard,fields-first`); the training changes that bring a 200-page scan under 2 minutes (page images first with the same windows for every job, a compact prompt, smaller images, a compact answer) wait for the next cycle.
- Code review of 2026-10-24: MinerU's turns are runs of at most 20 pages whatever the PDF's length (`ServedOcr.run_pages`, `split_pdf(run_pages=)`), so a document arriving mid full text waits about 25 s, not a whole document's OCR; a run waits for its turn at most `text_max_wait_s` (600 s) and then goes regardless (`GpuTurns.overdue_runs`), so fields arriving back to back cannot hold full texts back for ever. The turn is held from the start of a document's fields, rendering included: a run started while the pages render would still be reading when the model starts. `attach_full_text` judges the tier by the schema errors it adds, so fields that already break the schema keep their full text.
- Printed values only, at the gate too (requirement of 2026-10-24: no value the PDF does not print is served): before fields first serves the fields, MinerU reads only the pages they cite (`cited_pages`, `ServedOcr.ocr_text(pages=)`, `select_pages`; a few dozen of a 200-page policy, ~15-40 s, within the document's own GPU turn) and `verify_fields` holds a copy of the result to that text (`serving.printed_values.hold_result_to_printed`): a value not found there is held back as `<path>:pending_full_text`, not removed as printed nowhere - the model may have cited the wrong page - and `likely_missed` flags declarations fields left empty that those pages print. The model's own answer is kept, and when the remaining pages are read (the cited ones are not read again) it is held to the whole document: a held-back value printed elsewhere comes back, one printed nowhere is removed (`not_printed_removed`), as on the standard path. `attach_full_text` looks an image-only read over for missed fields too. Should the cited pages fail to be read, nothing is served before the full text; `verify=False` (`--unverified-fields`) serves unchecked fields flagged `fields:unverified`, for timing tests only.

## Changes of 2026-10-24 (printed values only and consistency)

- Printed values only (the user's decision of 2026-10-24: nothing invented in the served JSON): `serving/printed_values.py` holds every canonical policy to its pages after the merge and `conform`, with the OCR-tolerant `common.grounding.ground_tolerant`, on scans too. `printed_values.mode` in `configs/inference/vllm_serving.yaml`: `strict` (the default) takes a value printed nowhere out and flags it `<path>:not_printed_removed`, replacing `conform`'s `<path>:not_printed`; `flag` keeps it and flags it `<path>:not_printed`; any other value is refused. The record is `ExtractionResult.printed_check`; with no page text nothing is checked, flagged `printed_values:no_page_text`. Fields first runs the check in `attach_full_text` (`hold_result_to_printed`) once MinerU's text arrives (*Current implementation*, §6, acceptance).
- `ExtractionRequest.send_order` (`planned`, the default, or `reversed`; anything else is refused): the order a policy's windows go to the model, on both reading paths. The merge keeps planned order. The golden eval's repeat pass uses `reversed` to measure `run_to_run_agreement` (IMPL-08).

## Changes of 2026-10-24 (QA fixes)

- **Merge:** within its group, the window holding the declarations page (`PolicyWindow.leads`) merges first, so a declarations window split by its text or retried in halves still gives the declarations value (the half holding the declarations page inherits the lead; else the first half). A section in the wrong JSON type fails its window. Page references are held to the window's pages: one outside them is dropped and the value flagged `<path>:page_ref_outside_window`; "12" is page 12. `extract` refuses a request with no images or with `page_texts` not numbered 1..n; with no serving plan, a line with no schema is refused unless `allow_lob_fallback` is sent (then the base model against `_fallback.json`, flagged `route:lob_fallback_used`).
- **Printed values only, the rule:** a value is looked up on the cited pages that have text, then on every page with text. Found anywhere: printed. Not found, and every cited page has text (or it cites none): removed in strict mode. Not found while a cited page has no text (an image MinerU could not read): kept and flagged `<path>:unverified_unread_page`, counted as unchecked (record key `unread_page`). Unchecked only when no page has text. Codes match as one run of words, never pieces found apart; figures match whole, and a money amount only an amount-like printed figure (currency sign, separator or cents) - never a year or a code; two-character values match as whole tokens; yes/no readings and single characters stay unchecked; a `parsed` value behind a blank `raw` is looked for in its printed forms.
- **Removed rows:** ids are never renumbered after a removal (gaps are possible); a link to a removed row is dropped and flagged `link_to_removed_row`. A value removed with its row is named by its list and its position in the model's answer, `list[#N]`, so no flag names a served path that holds another value; `list_fields` follow the served JSON. A served row's id and links are the same at `fields_ready`, at `done` and on the standard path.
- **Fields first:** nothing is served when no cited page has text (`fields_verified` false, no `fields_ready`).
- **Transport:** redirects are refused (`common.transport.open_url`), so a token never follows one; a stream that ends with no finish reason and no `[DONE]` is `stream_cut`, cut off, and its window split; a stream broken mid-chunk is a `ModelRunnerError`. A page link's SAS must be a container or blob SAS (`sr=c`/`sr=b`, `sp=r`): an account SAS is refused. A transparent PNG is sent as it is on disk.
- A window's truncation message names its finish reason: the token limit, an answer stopped for repeating itself (`repetition`), or a stream that broke off (`stream_cut`).

## Changes of 2026-10-24 (architecture v2.7)

- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.7.docx`, which records the decisions of 2026-10-23 and 2026-10-24: printed values only, consistent output, fields first, one MinerU, production on Azure.

## Changes of 2026-10-25 (fast format profile, Azure service)

- Fast format profile: requests decode against the compact schema (`common.canonical.compact_schema`), and `_parse_generation` expands every answer to full `raw`/`parsed`/`page_ref` envelopes (`expand_answer`) before the shape check, the page check, the merge, the printed-values check and calibration, which never see the compact form; an invented value is removed as before. A window's groups are sent together (`_send_order`), so each after the first finds the pages' prefill in vLLM's prefix cache (on by default in vLLM V1; `generation.repeatable: true` turns it off). `send_order="reversed"` still merges in plan order. A release of the other profile is refused (`assert_prompt_hash`, `assert_window_plan_served`).
- `fields_first.read_fields(extract_options=)`: phase 1 reads with the release's calibrators and thresholds. `vllm_entrypoint.load_release_calibration(release, client)`: a release's calibrators without its adapter, used by `load_release_runtimes` and the Azure worker.
- The production service, `azure_service/` (`DEPLOY.md`): Azure Container Apps, an HTTPS API app and one worker app per GPU. `POST /v1/extractions` (PDF body or multipart, `lob`, optional HTTPS `webhook_url`) answers 202 with a job id; `GET /v1/extractions/{id}` the status and timings; `GET /v1/extractions/{id}/result` the checked fields from `fields_ready` (`final: false`) and the final JSON at `done`, 409 before. API keys per tenant (stored as digests, compared in constant time); size, page, PDF, line and webhook checks before anything is stored.
- Jobs: the Storage Queue message holds only the job id and tenant; status, result and the phase-1 checkpoint are whole-object JSON blobs in a jobs container (the PDF and page images are deleted when a job ends). The lease is renewed while a job runs; after `MAX_ATTEMPTS` deliveries a job fails and never loops; a restart resumes phase 2 from the checkpoint, or reads the document again when the worker died in phase 1. Webhooks on `fields_ready`, `done` and `failed`, signed `X-Extraction-Signature: sha256=<HMAC>` with a per-tenant secret, and carrying no document content.
- One worker replica per GPU: `GpuTurns` lives in the worker's process, so a second replica on the same GPU would run MinerU beside the other's fields. At start the worker checks the promoted release (`lora_on_base`, the MinerU pin, the prompt hash, the window plan, the base model) and that vLLM serves `policy-<release>`; the base-model fallback is off unless `ALLOW_BASE_FALLBACK` is set. `serving/vllm_entrypoint.py` is no longer the production entry point; its cold-start checks are reused. Not yet run against real Azure or a pod.

## Changes of 2026-10-26 (pipeline review fixes)

- New checks in the printed-values hold: raw re-parsed against its parsed value by declared type (`serving/raw_parsed.py`; strict nulls a clear mismatch, flag `parsed_mismatch`); a value printed only off its cited pages is kept and flagged `printed_off_cited_page`; `role_mismatch` when every line opening with the field's label prints another value; coverage `form_refs` items printed nowhere are removed; `row_not_colocated` (flag only) when a row's printed values share no page. `serving/value_flags.py` sends `printed_off_cited_page`, `role_mismatch`, `near_tie` and `parsed_mismatch` values to review.
- `near_tie` when a value's top-1 / top-2 margin is under `serving.near_tie_margin` (0.1 nats). The compact schema requires `r`, first, on yes/no envelopes (training already writes it).
- `ExtractionRequest.printed_texts`: the hold reads it instead of the page text the model was sent.
- Confidence: the mapped spans reach the feature builder; `ExtractionResult.confidence_features` and `calibrated_with` (not served) let fields first score confidence again on the text (`fields_first.rescore_on_text`) at the gate and at the end.
- More values go to review than before (off-page, role, near-tie, parse mismatches), and strict mode removes values whose parse contradicts their print. A day-first date with both parts 12 or less is read the US way.

## Changes of 2026-10-26 (gate and release decisions)

- Retention for review (#43), OFF by default: for tenants listed in `REVIEW_RETENTION_TENANTS` (after legal and contract sign-off), the Azure worker copies a `done` job's PDF, result and a manifest to the separate `REVIEW_CONTAINER` (`retained/<tenant>/<job>/`) before the PDF is deleted, when a value needs a person (`REVIEW_RETENTION_FLAG_KINDS`: low or no confidence, not printed, parse or role mismatch, off its cited page, near tie, likely missed, unread page, a link to a removed row, a row-count mismatch, a failed window; document-level flags never do), its confidence is below `REVIEW_RETENTION_LOW_CONFIDENCE` (0.80), or it falls in the `REVIEW_RETENTION_SAMPLE` (5%). Kept `REVIEW_RETENTION_DAYS` (90). `python -m azure_service.retention purge | export | mark-reviewed`: export copies documents into the labelling intake unlabelled (never a gold); a failed copy never fails a job. See azure_service/DEPLOY.md section 9.

---

# IMPL-12_testing_extraction_routine

# SPEC 12 — Testing / Extraction Routine (CLI Harness)

> Aligned with the code of 2026-10-23.

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-01, IMPL-02, IMPL-03 (OCR), IMPL-07 (inference core), IMPL-08 (metrics), IMPL-09 (calibration), IMPL-11 (serving pipeline).
>
> **Naming hazard (master §0.1):** the architecture's "**Fideon SPEC_12**" is the external multi-tenant deployment spec. This file is our testing harness. Different documents.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.7.docx` §17 (**extraction/testing routine**), §19 (folder placement), §13a (serving format: the adapter on the bf16 base), §5 (confidence without ground truth).

## Goal

A CLI harness to test any fine-tuned model version — or the base model with a training checkpoint applied — on real documents post-training, producing structured JSON + per-field/overall confidence + quality metrics, with version-organized outputs. The documents are ones the pipeline has already imported and OCR'd, read exactly as serving reads one (page images + MinerU page text). **It mirrors the exact production inference path (MinerU text → model → JSON) by reusing the serving pipeline (IMPL-11), so what you test is what you ship.**

## Deliverables

### 1. Folder layout under `testing/`
```
testing/
├── run_extraction.py            # CLI entrypoint (also reached as `orchestration.run extract`)
├── compare_models.py            # base vs base + adapter vs gold, per document (§6)
├── comparison.py                # field-by-field comparison, the all-fields report, Excel workbooks
├── render_prompts.py            # writes prompts/ from common.prompts
├── prompts/                     # reference renders of the prompts serving sends (generated)
│   ├── acord_25.prompt.txt, acord_125.prompt.txt, acord_140.prompt.txt
│   ├── lossrun.prompt.txt
│   ├── policy.prompt.txt, policy_<line>.prompt.txt               # + *.image_only.prompt.txt
│   └── policy_<line>.{decl,arrays,forms}.prompt.txt              # window prompts, common-model lines
├── ocr_cache/                   # page images fetched from processed/ in Blob, kept between runs
├── results/{version}/           # OUTPUT: canonical JSON per document, organized BY MODEL VERSION
│   └── v2/policy_0001.json
├── results/comparisons/         # compare_models runs, downloaded from Blob (§6)
├── metrics/{version}/           # OUTPUT: per-doc metrics + summary.json
└── test_data/                   # git-ignored; read by no code: documents enter by import + OCR
```
`extraction_registry.json` (§5) is appended by `run_batch` (through `append_registry`) after each document's
results and metrics are written; a document that failed writes no entry.

### 2. `testing/prompts/*.prompt.txt`
One per ACORD form (25, 125, 140), one for Loss Runs, one for the bare `policy` (a package policy, an unclassified line) and one per snapshot line (`common.schemas.SNAPSHOT_LINES`: the seven personal lines) — each in `ocr_plus_image` and `image_only` — plus, for every common-model line, one per section group (`decl`, `arrays`, `lineblk`): the window prompts serving actually sends. Each contains the role framing, the **injected JSON schema for that selection pulled from the IMPL-01 registry** (so it stays in sync), the extraction rules (null-handling, date formats, repeating table rows), and the modality-mode instruction line. They are generated (`python -m testing.render_prompts --write`), never hand-edited.

**Why one file per type, form and line (arch §17):** each has a different schema, different field semantics (a Loss Run's "valuation date" vs. an ACORD's "policy effective date"), and different edge cases. Separate files let a reviewer read exactly what each is asked, and one prompt can be tuned without risk to the others.

**These are the same prompts serving uses.** The harness does not read the files: it renders through `common.prompts` inside `serving.pipeline.extract`, as serving does. The files must resolve to the identical rendered prompt — `test_prompt_files_match_the_renderer` asserts this equality in a test rather than maintaining it by discipline.

**Canonical field mapping needs no change here** (master §1.4): the semantic glosses are schema `description` fields, so they propagate automatically through the existing schema injection. The one requirement is negative — **the extraction path must never import `common.aliases`**. Mapping surface labels to canonical keys is the model's job; a runtime lookup would cap the system at a hand-written list. `test_no_runtime_aliases` (IMPL-14) enforces this.

### 3. `testing/run_extraction.py` — the CLI

```bash
# User decides which model to run — base, v1, v2, v3, ... — on imported, OCR'd documents
python -m testing.run_extraction --model v2   --tenant T --split test --corpus v1
python -m testing.run_extraction --model base --tenant T --split test --corpus v1   # zero-shot baseline

# The base model with a training checkpoint applied per request, not merged
python -m testing.run_extraction --model base \
       --adapter /workspace/staging/adapters/scope/policy/v1/<run>/checkpoint-280 \
       --tenant T --split val --corpus v1

# Or through the operator command surface (IMPL-13), which wraps this:
python -m orchestration.run extract --model v2 --tenant T --source-ids policy_0001 policy_0002

# Image-only mode (no OCR text), to test the no-OCR pathway
python -m testing.run_extraction --model v2 --tenant T --source-ids policy_0001 --mode image_only

# Extract without scoring against the gold labels
python -m testing.run_extraction --model v2 --tenant T --split test --corpus v1 --no-score
```

- `--model` resolves via IMPL-02 `resolve_model_version` — **the user never has to know paths, just the tag.** Accepted values:

  | Value | Resolves to |
  |---|---|
  | `base` | Untuned `Qwen/Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct` at the pinned revision, **no adapter** |
  | `base` + `--adapter <folder>` | The base with that LoRA applied on every request, **not merged** — a `checkpoint-N` folder or a run's adapter folder, local or a Blob prefix (copied once to `/workspace/adapters`). Refused with any other `--model`, without `adapter_config.json`, above the engine's `max_lora_rank`, or with `enable_lora: false` |
  | `v1`, `v2`, … | The policy adapter's run of that version (`policy-v2`): the base with that version's adapter applied per request, staged or published — nothing is merged (decision 2026-10-23). A staged version loads the checkpoint its selection chose, else its latest checkpoint on the staging volume (with a warning), else it is refused. A version trained on another base than the configured `model_id@revision` is refused. For an older unified or Foundation run, its merged model plus a graduated per-type adapter where the routed type has one |
  | `v2` + `--format fp8` | Refused for a version of the policy adapter: it is a LoRA on the bf16 base, so there is no FP8 model. For an older run, that version's FP8 serving weights (refused while the version is still staged). A GGUF format here is a category error: llama.cpp loads those, and the endpoint runs vLLM |

  **`base` is not a curiosity** — it is the zero-shot baseline of the pilot protocol (IMPL-15) and the day-zero pre-annotation path (IMPL-04). Supporting it here means those two have one implementation, not three.
- **Which documents:** `--source-ids …` or `--split train|val|test` with `--corpus vN` (that corpus manifest's `source_ids_by_split`) — mutually exclusive; with neither, every labelled document of `--doc-type` (default `policy`) for `--tenant`. `--limit N` keeps the first N.
- `--mode ocr_plus_image` (default) | `image_only` — both production pathways testable from one harness.
- **Scoring is on by default**: a document with a gold label in `golden-labels/` is scored against it; one without, or whose gold has no review record (decision 2026-10-22), is extracted and not scored — the metrics file says why (`ground_truth: not scored: …`) and `summary.json` lists it under `not_scored`. `--no-score` extracts without comparing.
- Additional: `--label` (the name results are filed under; default the tag, or `base+<run>-<checkpoint>` with `--adapter`), `--format`, `--limit`, `--tenant`.

**What it does, step by step (arch §17):**
```
For each source_id:
  1. Read             → page count from processed/…/ocr_meta.json; every page image (cached in
                        ocr_cache/) and MinerU's per-page markdown as page_texts (none under
                        image_only); the label metadata (acord_form, lob) and the gold label
  2. Doc type         → known: --doc-type with the label's ACORD form (StaticClassifier);
                        the policy's line from the label metadata (classic auto → personal auto)
  3. Prompt select    → rendered by common.prompts for that type, form, line and window group
  4. Assemble input   → system = prompt (schema + instructions)
                        user   = [page image + page marker (+ OCR markdown, unless image_only)] per page
  5. Model inference  → the selected model generates JSON + token logprobs (policies: in windows over
                        every page, merged)
  6. Confidence       → per-field confidence from logprobs; no release calibrators are loaded, so the
                        identity transform applies and every field is flagged for review
  7. Validate         → serving's audit verdict (serving.pipeline.schema_verdict): JSON parses +
                        conforms to that selection's schema (strict_schema=False: an invalid
                        answer is kept and flagged schema:invalid)
  8. Write result     → results/{version}/{source_id}.json, with all_fields
  9. Metrics          → scored against the gold label → metrics/{version}/{source_id}.metrics.json
 10. Summarise        → metrics/{version}/summary.json; a failed document is listed, the batch carries on
```

**Steps 2–7 are executed by calling `serving/pipeline.py` (IMPL-11)** — the routine is a thin CLI wrapper over the serving pipeline plus document reading and metrics. It must **NOT re-implement inference**; doing so breaks test == prod, which is the entire point of the harness.

### 4. Metrics output

**Results `results/{version}/{doc}.json`** — the serving output contract (`ExtractionResult.as_dict()`: `extraction` is the answer — for a policy, the canonical JSON with every key of its line's schema — then `fields`, `list_fields`, `line_of_business`, `pages_used`, `review_flags`, `route`, and `reconciliation` for a Loss Run) with **`all_fields`** inserted after `list_fields`: every field of the selection's schema (`testing.comparison.field_report`) — those neither the answer nor the gold holds too — each with its `value` and `status` (`extracted`, `null`, `no_rows`, `not_in_output`, `system`, `structural`), a written value's `confidence` and `page_ref`, its review `flags`, and against a gold label its `gold` value and `result` (`correct`, `wrong`, `missed`, `invented`, `empty`); plus a `summary` of counts and the `document_flags`.

**Per-doc `metrics/{version}/{doc}.metrics.json`** (arch §17):
```json
{
  "document": "policy_0001",
  "model_version": "v2",
  "doc_type": "policy",
  "mode": "ocr_plus_image",
  "schema_valid": true,
  "overall_confidence": 0.88,
  "pages_used": [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9],
  "review_flags": ["coverages[3].limit:merge_conflict", "window_failed:arrays:p9"],
  "fields": {
    "policy.policy_number":   {"value": "...", "confidence": 0.94, "correct": true},
    "policy.expiration_date": {"value": "...", "confidence": 0.71, "correct": false}
  },
  "reference_accuracy": 0.91,
  "coverage_code_accuracy": 0.95,
  "field_exact_match_rate": 0.91,
  "field_normalized_match_rate": 0.93,
  "field_accuracy_by_field": {"...": "..."},
  "list_field_f1": {"coverages": 0.88},
  "list_field_recall": {"coverages": 0.83},
  "missed_rows": {"coverages": 2},
  "failures": [{"field": "policy.expiration_date", "expected": "...", "got": "..."}]
}
```
All correctness uses IMPL-08 metrics — `score_fields`, `score_all_list_fields`, and `values_agree` for each field's `correct` (dates, amounts and names normalised, numbers compared by their declared type) — the same definition of "correct" as the promotion gate. The gold label is narrowed to what the line's schema can hold (`common.canonical.schema_label`) and the system-filled fields are left out of both sides. On a **common-model line** both sides are narrowed, ids are compared through what their links name (`common.structural_ids.comparable_for`), table values are scored (`core_for_scoring`, as `run_eval.score_subset` does), and links, codes and overflow get numbers of their own (`CommonModelTally`: `reference_accuracy`, `coverage_code_accuracy`, `coverage_code_recall`, `additional_fields_recall`, `additional_fields_precision`). Without a gold label the file says `"ground_truth": "not supplied — confidence reported, accuracy not measurable"`.

**Batch summary `metrics/{version}/summary.json`** — aggregates across the batch for that model version: document count, schema-validity rate, mean confidence, mean latency per document, documents needing review, per-doc-type and per-mode counts, the failed documents with their errors, and — over the scored documents — mean field normalized match and mean list-field recall. **This is the file compared across model versions to answer "did v3 beat v2?"** — the testing-time mirror of the IMPL-08 promotion gate.

**On the confidence-without-ground-truth case (arch §17):** even with no golden JSON — a brand-new unknown document — the routine still emits `overall_confidence` and per-field confidence. That is the whole payoff of the calibration work: confidence is available at inference time on documents you have never labeled, which is exactly what routes low-confidence extractions to human review in production. (The harness loads no release calibrators, so its numbers are the identity transform of the raw signal, and every field is flagged; a release's calibrated confidence is measured by the golden eval, IMPL-08.)

### 5. `testing/extraction_registry.json`
One entry per extraction, so an output JSON is never ambiguous about its origin — written by `append_registry`, which `run_batch` calls after each document's results and metrics are written (§1):
```json
{
  "extractions": [
    {
      "document": "policy_0001",
      "doc_type": "policy",
      "model_version": "v2",
      "quant_format": null,
      "mode": "ocr_plus_image",
      "result_path": "results/v2/policy_0001.json",
      "metrics_path": "metrics/v2/policy_0001.metrics.json",
      "overall_confidence": 0.88,
      "schema_valid": true,
      "review_flags": [],
      "extracted_at": "2026-02-15T11:03:00Z"
    }
  ]
}
```
Combined with the `results/{version}/` layout, anyone can see at a glance that `results/v2/policy_0001.json` came from model v2 — from the path *and* the registry. `model_version` here is the same tag that resolves to a `run_manifest.json` (IMPL-02), so an extracted result traces all the way back to the corpus version and git commit that produced the model that generated it.

### 6. `testing/compare_models.py` — base vs base + adapter
```bash
python -m testing.compare_models --input policy.pdf --gold policy_gold.json --lob homeowners \
    --reviewer-id <reviewer> --review-date YYYY-MM-DD --adapter <checkpoint-N>
python -m testing.compare_models --input data/bundles/<document>/ --adapter <adapter folder>
python -m testing.compare_models --input <folder of document folders> --adapter <adapter folder>
```
- `--input` is a PDF (with `--gold` and `--lob`), a bundle folder (`document.pdf` + `golden.json` + `metadata.json`), or a folder of them. Each document is imported and OCR'd (in `/workspace/venv-ocr`) under its own tenant — `compare` by default, never `default` or the real data's — and a re-run finds it by checksum. A gold is compared only with a review record (decision 2026-10-22): a PDF's `--gold` takes `--reviewer-id` and `--review-date`, written into its `metadata.json`; a bundle folder carries its own. Without one the answers are compared with no gold.
- One model load (`load_model("base", adapter=…)`, and `without_adapter` for the base on the same engine); each document is extracted twice through `serving.pipeline.extract`, by the base alone and with the adapter applied, not merged. No calibrators: every field is flagged in both.
- Per document under `--out` (default `testing/results/comparisons/<label>-<stamp>/`): `document.pdf`, `gold.json`, `base.json`, `adapter.json` (canonical JSON, every schema key), `base.all_fields.json`, `adapter.all_fields.json`, and `comparison.xlsx` (Summary and Fields sheets: each field's gold, base and adapter values, results, and `fixed`/`broke`). Table rows are matched by their identifier (a VIN, a form number, a coverage name), not by position. Across documents: `summary.xlsx` (one row per document, pooled totals) and `errors.json` when a route failed.
- The finished folder is uploaded to Blob under `exports/{tenant}/comparisons/{run}/` (the raw container: it holds policy data), checked file by file, and removed from the pod (`--keep-on-pod` keeps it). The command prints the `azcopy` line that downloads it to the laptop, by default into `D:\Fine-Tuning\testing\results\comparisons` (git-ignored; `--download-to` changes it).

## Constraints
- Confidence emitted even without ground truth.
- **Same prompts and same pipeline as serving — reuse, never reimplement.**
- Documents are read from the OCR the pipeline wrote (`processed/`), produced by the pinned MinerU (IMPL-03); the corpus build refuses documents read by more than one MinerU. The harness does not re-check the pin.
- No PII in logs; `results/`, `metrics/`, `ocr_cache/` and `test_data/` are gitignored, and comparison runs leave the pod through the raw container only.

## Acceptance checklist
- [ ] `run_extraction --model v2 --split test --corpus v1` writes `results/v2/*.json`.
- [ ] `--model base` runs the untuned base model with no adapter and writes `results/base/*.json`.
- [ ] `--model base --adapter <checkpoint>` applies the LoRA per request without merging, and files results under `base+<run>-<checkpoint>` (or `--label`); `--adapter` with any other `--model` is refused.
- [ ] `orchestration.run extract` produces byte-identical output to calling `run_extraction.py` directly.
- [ ] `--mode image_only` runs with no OCR and still extracts.
- [ ] Per-field + overall confidence present **with and without** ground truth.
- [ ] `line_of_business` present in every result, with confidence, `null` when undetermined.
- [ ] With a gold label that has a review record, per-doc + summary metrics computed, including list-field recall; `--no-score` skips them; a gold with no review record is not scored, and the metrics file and `summary.json` (`not_scored`) say why.
- [ ] Every result carries `all_fields`: every field of its schema, with its value and, against gold, its result.
- [ ] `--model vN` loads the base with that version's adapter applied, not merged; a non-bf16 `--format` is refused for it (a GGUF format is a category error here: the harness runs vLLM). A staged version loads its selected checkpoint, else its latest one with a warning, else it is refused; a version trained on another base than the configured one is refused.
- [ ] `extraction_registry.json` links result → model version → run manifest.
- [ ] `testing/prompts/*.prompt.txt` render identically to `common.prompts` for the same inputs (asserted, not assumed).
- [ ] **Output equals the serving pipeline's output on the same input** (parity test).
- [ ] `compare_models` writes base and adapter JSON, both `all_fields` reports and `comparison.xlsx` per document, `summary.xlsx` across them, under its own tenant, and uploads the run to `exports/{tenant}/comparisons/`.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-21)

- Both entries run the extraction: `python -m testing.run_extraction …` and
  `python -m orchestration.run extract …`, which passes the same arguments to `run_extraction.main`. Extraction
  goes through `serving.pipeline.extract` — windows and the window merge for policies, the serving guards,
  the printed-values check, every-key output, dates in `MM/DD/YYYY` — so test output is production output.
- **Every page is read** (decision 2026-10-19, IMPL-11 §4): a policy of any length is read in windows over
  all its pages, never on selected pages, so `pages_used` lists every page and a long policy takes more
  windows than it did. Results written before 2026-10-21 read a policy longer than five pages on its selected
  pages only. Nothing in the harness selects pages: there is no page threshold and no `long_doc_types`.
- Documents are read from Blob as the pipeline wrote them (`document_request`): `processed/` page images and
  MinerU markdown, `golden-labels/` gold and metadata. A document not yet OCR'd is refused with what to run;
  one failing document is recorded in `summary.json` and the batch carries on (exit 1 only when every document
  failed).
- A policy's line comes from its label metadata (`lob`); without it the fallback canonical schema (the common
  model) is used. No serving plan is passed, so nothing is refused for its line.
- No calibrators are loaded: confidence is the identity transform and every field is flagged for review. This
  run measures extraction, not a release's review thresholds.
- `schema_valid` and the `schema:invalid` flag are serving's own verdict (`serving.pipeline.schema_verdict`, IMPL-11
  §5): on a common-model line the served answer, every key filled, is what is judged.
- The gold is read from `golden-labels/` under the form-edition rule where it holds — a common-model policy
  line (`document_request` → `label_rules.under_edition_rule`) — as the gate's golden eval reads the frozen set
  (IMPL-08): the served answer there has a form's edition moved out of its number into `edition_date` (IMPL-11),
  and a gold imported before that rule is not rewritten in Blob, so scored as stored it would count the
  convention wrong. A self-contained line's gold is scored as stored: nothing on it moves an edition.
- A gold with no review record (`export_golden_labels.review_problem`, decision 2026-10-22; a twin inherits its
  seed's) is not scored: `document_request` returns no gold and logs why, the extraction still runs and is
  written, the metrics file records `ground_truth: not scored: …`, and `summary.json` lists it under
  `not_scored`. `compare_models` reads golds the same way.
- On the pod `run_extraction` and `compare_models` run detached in tmux like every long job; they survive a
  closed laptop.
- The gate's numbers do not come from this harness: they come from the frozen golden eval set
  (IMPL-08 `golden_eval.evaluate_version`), through the same serving pipeline.

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- Added the alignment line and dated *Current implementation* 2026-10-13.
- Goal and §1: documents are imported and OCR'd ones read from Blob, not PDFs dropped into `test_data/` (now unread); added `compare_models.py`, `comparison.py`, `render_prompts.py`, `results/comparisons/`, `summary.json`; the registry file is not written by the CLI.
- §2: real prompt file names (per ACORD form, per line, image-only variants, window prompts per group); the harness renders through `common.prompts` rather than reading the files.
- §3: replaced `--input`/`--ground-truth` with `--source-ids` / `--split` + `--corpus` / `--tenant` / `--doc-type` / `--no-score` / `--label`; added `--model base --adapter <checkpoint>`; corrected `--format` (FP8 serving weights, not GGUF); rewrote the step list (read from Blob, static classification, identity calibration, `strict_schema=False`, all_fields, summary).
- §4: results JSON carries `all_fields`; per-doc metrics keys as written (typed `values_agree`, common-model scoring and tally metrics); summary is `summary.json` with the aggregates actually computed (no ECE or per-value LoB accuracy).
- §5: noted that `append_registry` is not called by `run_batch`.
- §6 (new): `compare_models` — inputs, compare tenant, outputs (`base/adapter.json`, `*.all_fields.json`, `comparison.xlsx`, `summary.xlsx`), upload to `exports/{tenant}/comparisons/{run}/` and the azcopy download.
- Constraints and acceptance checklist updated to match.
- `run_batch` now writes `extraction_registry.json` (commit f5668a4).

## Changes since 2026-10-13

- Header: aligned with the code of 2026-10-21; architecture reference `finetuning-architecture-v2.4.docx`.
- §3: a policy is read in windows over every page; validation is serving's audit verdict (`schema_verdict`).
- §4: the `pages_used` example lists every page.
- §5: `run_batch` writes the registry (the stale "does not call today" removed).
- *Current implementation*: every page read, no page selection, results before 2026-10-21 read selected pages; `schema_valid` is serving's verdict; golds read under the form-edition rule, as the golden eval reads them.

## Changes of 2026-10-22 (code review)

- §2 and *Current implementation*: a gold with no review record (decision 2026-10-22) is extracted and not scored, and the metrics file and `summary.json` (`not_scored`) say why.
- *Current implementation*: golds are read under the form-edition rule only on common-model policy lines (`label_rules.under_edition_rule`).
- §6: `compare_models --reviewer-id --review-date` give a PDF's `--gold` its review record; without one the answers are compared with no gold.
- Acceptance checklist: scoring needs a reviewed gold.
- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.5.docx` and the code of 2026-10-22.

## Changes of 2026-10-22 (one adapter)

- One adapter (decision 2026-10-22): `--model vN` resolves the policy adapter's run (before, only unified/Foundation runs were found); the adapter path example is `scope/policy/`; the line prompts snapshotted are those of `SNAPSHOT_LINES`.

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (full text)

- Each document's metrics carry `text_coverage` (the share of its page text's words found in the served JSON, SPEC_21's every-word rule); `summary.json` adds `mean_text_coverage`, `min_text_coverage`, `documents_below_full_text` (under `FULL_TEXT_COVERAGE` = 0.99, chosen, not measured) and `documents_without_full_text` (flagged `text_sections:no_page_text`). The full-text tier is never scored: scoring narrows the answer to the model view, which has no `text_sections`.
- `testing/extract_server.py` (pod) and `testing/extract_client.py` (laptop): a PDF posted through the pod's RunPod proxy URL is OCR'd by MinerU in the OCR environment (`data_pipeline/ocr/ocr_worker.py`, kept loaded) and read by `serving.pipeline.extract` with the base model or `--adapter`; jobs answered at once and polled (the proxy's ~100 s limit), one at a time, token-guarded; each reports pages, windows planned, model calls, OCR/model/total seconds, schema validity, review flags by kind and `text_coverage`. `EXTRACT_SERVER_TOKEN` in `.env.example`.
- `testing/latency_check.py`: times the extraction routine on PDFs against a model already served (`vllm serve`, `inference_core/openai_backend.py`: one chat request per window, run concurrently; page images as base64 data URLs; the schema slice as `response_format`; the end-of-turn token dropped from the scored tokens) and a served MinerU 4 API (`data_pipeline/ocr/mineru_api.py`: `/v1/tasks`, `structured_content` written per page as the OCR stage writes it). Reports render, OCR (and the server's own OCR seconds), model and total seconds, model calls and MB sent per document; run it next to the servers, since page images go with every window. `FIDEON_REMOTE_TOKEN` in `.env.example`. A served MinerU 4 is not the corpus pin (3.4.5): for timing and checking, not training data.

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (served model speed)

- `data_pipeline/ocr/served_ocr.py` (`ServedOcr`): renders a PDF's pages in several processes (`render_pdf_pages(..., workers=)`, the same bytes as one process) while a served MinerU reads it, optionally as several runs of pages at once (`ocr_via_api(..., parts=)`, `split_pdf`); writes `page_NNN.png`/`.md`; also the job server's OCR call.
- `testing/latency_check.py` reads through it, sends each window as it can (`--concurrency`, default 128), stops repeating answers (`--no-repeat-stop` to compare), can send images by path (`--images file`) or ask for a served adapter (`--served-model`), and scores each document against its gold JSON when one is found (`--gold-dir`, or `Gold_json` beside the PDF's folder; `testing.run_extraction.score_against_ground_truth`). Each document's JSON carries `window_stats` and a `windows` summary (rounds, splits, failures, cut off, stopped repeating, prompt and answer tokens, the slowest window); the table adds pages-and-text seconds, rounds, splits, answer tokens, the slowest window and exact match.
- `testing/extract_server.py` can use the pod's own servers (`--model-url`, `--mineru-url`, `--remote-token`, `--images`, `--tier`, `--ocr-parts`, `--served-model`): it then loads neither vLLM nor MinerU, and only the PDF crosses the proxy. Its summary adds `windows` and the rendering/OCR timings; the result JSON adds `window_stats`.

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (page bytes)

- `testing/latency_check.py` from a laptop through the proxy: gray pages go as grayscale PNGs (the same pixels to the model; `--color-pages` to compare); `--images link` uploads each page once to Blob while MinerU reads the PDF - after the PDF is at MinerU, so the two do not share the connection (`ServedOcr.read(publish_pages=)`, `ocr_via_api(uploaded=)`) - and sends the model read-only links (`--link-token` / `$PAGE_LINK_SAS_TOKEN`, checked at start; `--keep-links`); `--ocr-parts N` uploads the runs of pages one after another, each polled from the moment it is in, so MinerU reads the first while the rest upload; `--mineru-token` when MinerU's token is not the model's (a RunPod serverless endpoint's is the API key). `Sent MB` is now everything that left: the PDF, the page links and the model's requests (`sent_mb_parts`).

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (incremental training)

- Nothing is merged (decision 2026-10-23): `--model vN` is the base with that version's adapter applied per request (`query_registry` kind `base_plus_adapter`), and a non-bf16 `--format` is refused for it (§3 table, the registry example, acceptance).
- Code review of the incremental training: `--model vN` refuses a version trained on another base than the configured one, and a staged version loads its selected checkpoint, else its latest with a warning, else it is refused (§3 table, acceptance).

## Changes of 2026-10-23 (architecture v2.6)

- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.6.docx`, which now records the one policy adapter, one version per cycle, incremental training and no merge or quantization.

## Changes of 2026-10-24 (one served MinerU)

- `testing/latency_check.py` and `testing/extract_server.py` read with the pinned MinerU (`configs/shared/ocr.yaml`): `--tier` defaults to the pinned tier, every task's version and tier are checked, and `--any-mineru` accepts another (timing only); `latency_check` refuses a `--tier` other than the pinned one without it. `--ocr-mode` defaults to the OCR stage's rule, per document, rather than MinerU's `auto`.

## Changes of 2026-10-24 (fields first)

- `testing/latency_check.py --runs standard,fields-first` reads each document both ways, one row each (`time_fields_first`): `fields` (the gate) and `full_text` (when the tier joined) next to the standard run's time, with exact match against gold for both - the measurement that decides whether image-only fields can be the production path.
- `testing/extract_server.py --fields-first` (needs `--mineru-url`): a job becomes `fields_ready` once the model has read the page images (its result already written, `/result` answers), and a second worker adds MinerU's text in the GPU's idle turns before `done`; a failed full text leaves the fields standing, reported as the job's error. `ExtractService.submit` takes the server's default mode. `testing/extract_client.py` waits through `fields_ready`, says when each document's fields came, and reports `Fields s`.
- The window summary adds `first_token_s_max` (windows queued, or put back for want of memory) and `by_group`: each job's answer lengths (median, p95, longest), to set the answer limits from.
- Code review of 2026-10-24: `latency_check` scores a fields-first document twice - when the fields come (`accuracy_at_fields`, the `Exact match at fields` column: what the gate serves) and as finally served, after the page text could change it. The job server writes each result whole (`write_whole`: a temporary file swapped in), since a `fields_ready` result is served while its full text is added; takes `--run-pages` (20) and `--text-max-wait` (600 s); keeps a job's fields for its second phase only once its first has succeeded. The client reports a document whose fields came but whose full text failed, and exits 1 for it. From the laptop with `--images link`, fields first puts the page upload before the model (nothing to hide it behind, unlike OCR): about 1.5-2 minutes for 114 pages at 1 MB/s; negligible on Azure.
- Fields first serves its fields only once held to the pages they cite (IMPL-11): the job server reports `fields_served`, `fields_verified` and `cited_pages`, becomes `fields_ready` only with checked fields, and a job whose full text then fails is `done` with its checked fields, or `failed` when none were served. `latency_check` scores the fields as served at the gate (held-back values included) and the final result, counts `fields_check.held_back`, and times `verify` inside `fields`; `--unverified-fields` scores unchecked fields, for timing only. Tests: a value cited on the wrong page is held back, then served once found; an invented value is never served; fields whose pages cannot be read are not served before the full text.

## Changes of 2026-10-24 (printed values only and consistency)

- Test outputs are served outputs, so they hold printed values only (IMPL-11; the user's decision of 2026-10-24): in strict mode, the default, a value no page prints is null in `extraction` and in `all_fields`, flagged `<path>:not_printed_removed`; against gold, a removed value the gold holds counts as missed. `printed_values.mode: flag` in `configs/inference/vllm_serving.yaml` keeps such values and flags them `<path>:not_printed` instead. What the check found (`ExtractionResult.printed_check`) is not written to the results JSON.
- Fields first: the fields served at `fields_ready` are held to the pages they cite (`serving.fields_first.verify_fields`, IMPL-11); a value not found on them yet is held back (`pending_full_text`), and the whole document is checked when MinerU's full text arrives, before the full-text tier. So `latency_check`'s `Exact match at fields` scores the fields as first served, and its final score the fully checked ones.

## Changes of 2026-10-24 (QA fixes)

- The served MinerU client's QA fixes (IMPL-03) apply to the test runners; a non-pinned `--tier` is refused unless `--any-mineru` (the job server too). See IMPL-11 for the merge, printed-values and transport fixes.
- `extract_client`: a Status column (`done`, `fields served, full text failed: ...`, `FAILED: ...`, `NOT SENT: ...`); a timing the server did not report is left blank, never a crash; `summary.*` is always written; exit 1 unless every document was read in full. Lines and PDF sizes are checked before anything is uploaded (`/health` reports `max_pdf_bytes`); the documents already queued are still collected after a refusal; `--url` must be HTTPS or HTTP to this machine, redirects are refused, and server errors are reported, not raised. Output names never clash (`-2` added; `summary` reserved). The job server answers 401 for a non-ASCII token and 400 for a bad Content-Length, lists its jobs under a lock, and refuses a non-pinned `--tier` and plain-HTTP server URLs before the detach. `latency_check` canonicalises each `--doc` line and refuses an unknown one, refuses a run named twice and `--concurrency` below 1, and checks the URLs and the link login before the detach (`setup_problem`, against the environment the run will have).

## Changes of 2026-10-24 (architecture v2.7)

- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.7.docx`, which records the decisions of 2026-10-23 and 2026-10-24: printed values only, consistent output, fields first, one MinerU, production on Azure.

## Changes of 2026-10-26 (fast format shipped)

- `testing.render_prompts` renders a fast-format window's reference file as what the model reads: the shared system prompt, a line standing for the window's pages, then the group's request (`render_group_block`). The policy window references under `testing/prompts/` are regenerated in the fast format.

## Changes of 2026-10-26 (pipeline review fixes)

- `latency_check --repeat` reads every document twice, interleaved, on the server path and writes `run_to_run.json`.

# IMPL-13_orchestration

# SPEC 13 — Orchestration (Command Surface + RunPod Controller + Pipeline DAG)

> Aligned with the code of 2026-10-23.

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: all prior specs (01–12).
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.7.docx` §0e (decisions of 2026-10-19), §13 (**end-to-end pipeline, 11 stages, idempotency, the gate**), §13c (**operator command surface**), §14 (**RunPod: ephemeral training pods vs. persistent serving endpoint**), §12 (every cycle recorded), §17 (extraction routine), §11a (sweep orchestration — deferred).

## Goal

Expose the whole lifecycle through **three operator commands plus one umbrella command**, so a cycle is run by a person who knows the version tag and nothing else about paths, pods, or stage wiring.

## 1. The command surface

Single entrypoint, `orchestration/run.py`, with four lifecycle subcommands (plus `promote`, `versions`, `deploy-endpoint`, `rollback-endpoint` and `freeze-eval-set`; see §4 and *Current implementation*). Every cycle trains the one policy adapter (`common/adapter.py`; decision 2026-10-22) and takes the next version, which names its run (`policy-v3`), adapter and release:

| # | Command | Covers arch §13 stages | Ends with |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | `finetune` | 1 → 6 (ingest, OCR, labeling, dataset build, train, checkpoint eval) | The adapter on the **RunPod staging volume** |
| **2** | `package` | 7 → 9 (calibrate, **gate**, bundle and push) | A promoted **release bundle** in Azure Blob; the release it replaces archived |
| **3** | `extract` | §17 extraction routine | Extracted JSON + confidence + metrics, locally |
| **—** | `all` | `finetune` then `package` | Same as command 2 |

**`all` never includes `extract`.** Extraction is a separate concern — you run it against a chosen model version whenever you want, including against models trained weeks earlier and against the untuned base. Folding it into the build would conflate "produce a model" with "use a model."

```bash
# 1 — ingest through checkpoint selection. The adapter is staged on RunPod, not yet in Blob.
#     A new cycle takes the next version itself and says so ("this cycle is v3"),
#     and continues the tenant's promoted adapter unless it has to start from the base.
python -m orchestration.run finetune --input ./intake

# start from the base model instead (after a data deletion, or to reset drift)
python -m orchestration.run finetune --input ./intake --fresh

# resume a cycle, or rerun one that failed: name its version
python -m orchestration.run finetune --version v3 --from-stage training

# 2 — calibrate, gate, then publish and promote the release bundle (releases/{tenant}/v3/).
python -m orchestration.run package \
       --version v3 \
       [--fixes-defect "<ticket>"]          # only when the release exists to fix a defect

# the registry, and a rollback
python -m orchestration.run versions
python -m orchestration.run promote --version v2 --by "<person>"

# 3 — extraction on imported, OCR'd documents, model chosen by the operator.
python -m orchestration.run extract --model base --tenant T --split test --corpus v2
python -m orchestration.run extract --model v2   --tenant T --source-ids policy_0001 policy_0002
python -m orchestration.run extract --model base --adapter <staged checkpoint-N> --tenant T --split val --corpus v2

# all — commands 1 and 2 back to back. Extraction excluded by design.
python -m orchestration.run all --input ./intake
```

Each subcommand is a thin argument-parsing shell over `pipeline_dag.py` stage functions (§4). No orchestration logic lives in the CLI layer.

## 2. `finetune` — command 1 (stages 1 → 6)

**`finetune` produces artifacts; `package` judges and publishes them.** The gate moved into `package` under arch v2.1 §13, because it scores what is served, with the calibrators `package` fits first. Since 2026-10-23 what is served is the base with the selected adapter applied: nothing is merged or quantized.

Runs, in order: **ingest → OCR → labeling → dataset build → train (the ONE policy adapter, continuing the promoted release unless it starts from the base) → select checkpoint.**

**The human-labeling precondition.** Stage 3 (labeling) is human work and cannot be inside an automated command. `finetune` therefore:

1. Ingests and OCRs **everything** in `--input` (OCR in practice runs first, in the OCR environment; see *Current implementation*).
2. Builds the corpus from **only the source_ids that have a validated `golden.json`** (IMPL-04) — and refuses the run, naming them, if any gold has no review record (`load_labeled_documents`; no real `reviewer_id` and `review_date` in its metadata, a twin's inherited from its seed; decision 2026-10-22).
3. **Reports the unlabeled backlog** — a count and a `source_id` list written to the run report — and continues with what is labeled.
4. Aborts before training only if the labeled set is empty, or below `--min-labels-per-type` (default 25, matching the day-zero rule in IMPL-04).

This is the honest shape: you ingest 500 documents, 200 are labeled, you train on 200, and the command tells you 300 are waiting for a reviewer.

**The corpus is refused before anything trains on too little of it.** `stage_dataset_build` refuses, with nothing written, a corpus that set aside more than 25% of its loaded documents (`FAIL_REJECTED_SHARE`, measured by `corpus_set_aside` — the same helper preflight's corpus check reads), a corpus missing a split it needs, and documents read by more than one MinerU version.

**No training fan-out.** One `finetune` run launches **one** training job: the policy adapter, a LoRA on the bf16 base over all the policy data so far, new and existing (decision 2026-10-22). There are no training scopes and no layout families (Fideon SPEC_09's five adapter groups are knowingly not followed); `--scope` and `run_multi_scope` are gone. v1 launched 1 + N sequentially — a Foundation, then one adapter per document type stacked on it — and that topology is unservable: vLLM applies one LoRA per request, so the two could never both be active (arch v2.1 §4.1). A per-type adapter returns only through the §4.2 graduation gate, trained on the merged foundation and never stacked.

**Continue or start fresh** (`pipeline_dag.continuation`; the user's decision of 2026-10-23). A cycle continues the tenant's promoted release: its adapter's weights, with a fresh optimizer and learning-rate schedule (ms-swift `adapters`), on the original base model — never a merged model. It starts from the base instead when `--fresh` is given (after a data deletion, or to reset drift), nothing is promoted, the parent's run manifest cannot be read, it trained on another base model, it has another LoRA rank, alpha or target modules, its adapter is not in Blob (`adapter_config.json` or `adapter_model.safetensors` missing), its `adapter_config.json` cannot be read or was saved with other LoRA settings than the config (`training.train.adapter_config_mismatch`: dropout, rsLoRA and bias as well as rank, alpha and target modules), its release has no bundle, or its release was packaged with another prompt hash or window plan (a template, schema or section-map change), or its eval report is missing or on another yardstick (the set frozen again, or other scoring code), which the gate's line rule would block only after training. The promoted release's bundle is read by its release id (`promoted_release`), so a release with an old-style name (`release-YYYY.M.N`) is continued too. The reason is logged and recorded on the run manifest (`fresh_reason`) and in the training stage's result (`continued`, `fresh_reason`); the parent is `RunManifest.parent_run_id`, also in the registry index row. The parent's adapter is copied to staging (`parent-adapters/policy/vN`). A continued run uses `continued_training`'s non-null values (IMPL-06). A continued run that stopped part-way resumes its own checkpoint; the run fingerprint names the parent and the weights it starts from (IMPL-06). The first cycle on the new training set and canonical schema will start fresh (the prompt hash changes); later ones continue.

**What the operator should know about continuing:**
- Old documents train again every cycle, so over many cycles they are seen more often than new ones — a memorisation risk. Fewer epochs or a lower rate for continued cycles (`continued_training`, set from a measured continued run) is the lever, and the gate on the frozen set catches what it costs.
- A wrong label, learned once, carries forward until it is fixed in the data.
- Reproducing a version needs its chain of parents (`parent_run_id`).
- Start fresh (`--fresh`) after a data deletion and from time to time to reset drift; a schema, prompt, LoRA-settings or base-model change starts fresh by itself, and so does a change to the scoring code or a set frozen again. The scoring code changed on 2026-10-24, so a cycle continuing a release scored before then starts from the base instead; none is promoted yet.

**The version.** A new `finetune` or `all` takes the next version itself: one more than the highest number used by the adapter's runs (the registry index) or by any tenant's corpora (`corpus/{tenant}/vN`) and releases (`releases/{tenant}/vN`) (`registry_utils.releases.next_cycle_version`, `artifact_registry.paths.next_version`). One sequence for every tenant: runs, weights and eval reports are not kept per tenant, so two tenants on one number would write one run's artifacts over the other's (before the code review of 2026-10-23 only this tenant's corpora and releases counted). `--version vN` names one only to resume (`--from-stage`, which refuses to run without it: the next free number after a first attempt that wrote anything would be a new cycle) or to rerun a failed cycle. The corpus defaults to the cycle's version (`--corpus-version` names one already built).

**Where the gate's numbers come from.** The gate (in `package`) reads the candidate's scores from the adapter's frozen-eval-set report (`eval-reports/v{n}/scope/policy/summary.json`, IMPL-08) — producing it, when absent, by serving the frozen set through the serving pipeline with the base and the selected adapter applied (as served) and the release's bf16 calibrators, each document's main mode read a second time for `run_to_run_agreement` (`run_to_run_check`, on) — and the baseline from this tenant's promoted release's own report: the release marked `promoted` in the tenant's release index (`pipeline_dag.promoted_version`). Before 2026-10-22 it read a run-manifest status `promoted` that no real run ever set, so every candidate was gated as a first version. The frozen set is scored on the lines the release serves only (`evaluate_version(lines=served_lines(ctx))`; the report records `lines_scored`): serving sends every other policy — another line, a package, no line — to the base model. A run with no trained line fails the gate step before the model loads. A candidate report is reused only when its `eval_set` is this tenant's and it was scored on the same lines and the same checkpoint (`adapter_checkpoint`: the selected one, else the staged adapter directory); otherwise the frozen set is scored again. Each report also keeps every document's scores and weights (`per_document`, `per_document_weights`) and the frozen set it was scored on (`eval_set`, IMPL-08); `paired_document_scores` pairs the two on the documents both scored, so non-inferiority is a **paired bootstrap**, weighted so it tests the metric the report states. It pairs only when both reports are on one yardstick: the same frozen set of this tenant, scored by the same code (`scoring_fingerprint`). A baseline on another yardstick — another frozen set, another tenant, other scoring code (`scoring_fingerprint`), or a report that does not record its yardstick — is not compared: the candidate is judged on the floors alone, without the improvement rule (decision 2026-10-22). So is a promoted report scored on other lines than the candidate serves, or without its calibrators (`calibrated: false`, as an ad-hoc `run_eval` writes it to the same key). A promoted `release-YYYY.M.N` row whose adapter is not a `policy-vN` run is no baseline (a warning), rather than the policy adapter's report at that number. On one yardstick, reports that cannot be paired — no shared documents, no per-document scores — are compared on point estimates, the improvement rule cannot be enforced, and the decision's warnings say so and why. Neither report is optional and neither is fabricated: with no report a dry run fails rather than gating on nothing, and with no promoted version the baseline is genuinely `None` — a first version — rather than a lookup that quietly failed.

**The gate is a hard stop inside `package`.** It runs after calibrate (arch v2.1 §13; there is no merge or quantize stage since 2026-10-23), so a regression stops the **release**, not the build: the adapter is trained and selected, nothing is packaged, and the staged artifacts sit on a volume that will be reclaimed. It exits non-zero with the per-metric verdicts — floor, interval and basis, not a bare delta. A passing release is classed **production** (field normalized match ≥ 0.92) or **interim** — below that, or whenever the release was scored on fewer than 150 real frozen policies (twins do not count, decision 2026-10-22), with a warning. The count is the real documents the candidate was scored on, of the lines it serves (`eval_set_size`: the candidate report's own real counts, else the frozen manifest's `real_documents_by_doc_type`; an older manifest without them is unknown, so interim), not the whole set's `small_set_accepted`. The `tier` is recorded in the gate decision and the bundle.

**A continued run is held, line by line, to the runs before it** (decision 2026-10-23; IMPL-08). Each line the candidate serves is held to the best run on it since the last run from the base: the parent, its parent and so on, down `parent_run_id`. The best run has the highest weighted field match on the line's real documents that both reports scored, with at least 5 such documents (`gating.LINE_MIN_DOCUMENTS`); on a tie, the nearer run. A paired bootstrap over those documents blocks the line (gate `line:<line>`) unless the lower bound of the 95% interval of (candidate − reference) is above −0.015 (`DEFAULT_DELTA`). A line with fewer shared documents, a new line, and a run with no line to compare get a warning only. A line the parent's release served that the candidate no longer serves blocks at `line:<line>`. No comparable parent report — missing, on another frozen set or scoring code, or without `per_document_lines` — blocks (gate `line_regression_evidence`); the remedy is the next cycle from the base (`--fresh`, without `--version`), or keeping the parent's report on the frozen set. A small line has a wide interval, so it may block until it has more documents; it can be waived as `line:<line>`. The usual comparison with the promoted release still applies. A run continued by hand from a directory (`training.train --continue-from`) names no parent and is blocked.
